\textstyle{\mathcal{F}_{xz}\times\mathcal{F}_{zy}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{c}
\textstyle{\mathcal{F}_{xw}\times\mathcal{F}_{wy}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{c}
\textstyle{\mathcal{F}_{xy}}

We will often write
\mathcal{F}_{x_{0}\ldots x_{i}}
as shorthand for
\mathcal{F}_{x_{0}x_{1}}\times\ldots\times\mathcal{F}_{x_{i-1}x_{i}}
. These are faces of
\mathcal{F}_{x_{0}x_{i}}
, and any face of
\mathcal{F}_{x_{0}x_{i}}
is a union of components of these. In other words, there is a system of faces for
\mathcal{F}_{xy}
given by

\mathcal{F}_{x_{0}\ldots x_{i}}

for
x_{0}=x,\ldots,x_{i}=y
in
\mathcal{F}
; similarly each
\mathcal{F}_{xx^{\prime\prime}}
has a system of boundary faces given by the
\mathcal{F}_{xx^{\prime}x^{\prime\prime}}
as
x^{\prime}
varies over
\mathcal{F}
.

A
system of collars
on a flow category
\mathcal{F}
consists of a system of collars
\mathcal{C}_{xy}
on all
\mathcal{F}_{xy}
, restricting to the product system of collars on
\mathcal{F}_{xz}\times\mathcal{F}_{zy}
induced by Lemma
34
from
\mathcal{C}_{xz}
and
\mathcal{C}_{zy}
.

A
collared flow category
is a flow category
\mathcal{F}
equipped with a system of collars.

Note that in particular, a flow category forms a (non-unital) topological category.

It follows from Lemmas
34
and
35
that

Lemma 47
.

Any flow category admits a system of collars, which is unique up to isotopy.

Given this, we will often choose systems of collars on flow categories and denote them
\mathcal{C}
, without explicitly stating that we do so, and we will generally omit the word collared.

{ex}

Given a Morse function
f
on a closed manifold
M
and a suitably generic metric
g
, Wehrheim
[
Weh12
]
constructs a flow category
\overline{\mathcal{M}}^{f}
with objects critical points of
f
, gradings given by the Morse indices, and
\overline{\mathcal{M}}^{f}_{xy}
given by compactified moduli spaces of gradient flow lines between any two given critical points.

{ex}

Let
M
be a closed manifold of dimension
n
. There is a flow category
\mathcal{F}
with two objects
x
and
y
, with
|x|=n+1
,
|y|=0
,
\mathcal{F}_{xy}=M
and no other data.

Example 4.1
.

Let
\mathcal{F}
be a flow category with three objects
x,y,z
, with
|x|>|y|>|z|
. Let
m=|x|-|y|
and
n=|y|-|z|
. Then the manifold data of
\mathcal{F}
consists of a closed
(m-1)
-manifold
\mathcal{F}_{xy}
, a closed
(n-1)
-manifold
\mathcal{F}_{yz}
, and a nullbordism
\mathcal{F}_{xz}
of the product of these closed manifolds.

Definition 48
.

*
is the flow category with one object (which we also call
*
), graded in degree 0. This admits a canonical system of collars.

For a flow category
\mathcal{F}
, we write
\mathcal{F}[i]
for the flow category which is the same as
\mathcal{F}
, except all the gradings are increased by
i
. Explicitly, this means
|\cdot|^{\mathcal{F}[i]}=|\cdot|^{\mathcal{F}}+i
.

A
morphism
of flow categories
\mathcal{W}:\mathcal{F}\rightarrow\mathcal{G}
consists of compact smooth manifolds with faces
\mathcal{W}_{xy}
of dimension
|x|-|y|
for
x
in
\mathcal{F}
and
y
in
\mathcal{G}
, along with maps

c=c^{\mathcal{W}}:\mathcal{F}_{xz}\times\mathcal{W}_{zy}\rightarrow\mathcal{W}_{xy}

and

c=c^{\mathcal{W}}:\mathcal{W}_{xz}\times\mathcal{G}_{zy}\rightarrow\mathcal{W}_{xy}

which are suitably associative and compatible with the
c^{\mathcal{F}}
and
c^{\mathcal{G}}
(in the sense that the following diagrams commute:

\textstyle{\mathcal{F}_{xx^{\prime}}\times\mathcal{F}_{x^{\prime}x^{\prime\prime}}\times\mathcal{W}_{x^{\prime\prime}y}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{c^{\mathcal{W}}}
\scriptstyle{c^{\mathcal{F}}}
\textstyle{\mathcal{F}_{xx^{\prime}}\times\mathcal{W}_{x^{\prime}y}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{c^{\mathcal{W}}}
\textstyle{\mathcal{F}_{xx^{\prime\prime}}\times\mathcal{W}_{x^{\prime\prime}y}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{c^{\mathcal{W}}}
\textstyle{\mathcal{W}_{xy}}

\textstyle{\mathcal{F}_{xx^{\prime}}\times\mathcal{W}_{x^{\prime}y^{\prime}}\times\mathcal{G}_{y^{\prime}y}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{c^{\mathcal{W}}}
\scriptstyle{c^{\mathcal{W}}}
\textstyle{\mathcal{F}_{xx^{\prime}}\times\mathcal{W}_{x^{\prime}y}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{c^{\mathcal{W}}}
\textstyle{\mathcal{W}_{xy^{\prime}}\times\mathcal{G}_{y^{\prime}y}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{c^{\mathcal{W}}}
\textstyle{\mathcal{W}_{xy}}

\textstyle{\mathcal{W}_{xy^{\prime\prime}}\times\mathcal{G}_{y^{\prime\prime}y^{\prime}}\times\mathcal{G}_{y^{\prime}y}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{c^{\mathcal{G}}}
\scriptstyle{c^{\mathcal{W}}}
\textstyle{\mathcal{W}_{xy^{\prime\prime}}\times\mathcal{G}_{y^{\prime\prime}y}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{c^{\mathcal{W}}}
\textstyle{\mathcal{W}_{xy^{\prime}}\times\mathcal{G}_{y^{\prime}y}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{c^{\mathcal{W}}}
\textstyle{\mathcal{W}_{xy}}

which define a system of faces

\mathcal{F}_{x_{0}\ldots x_{i}}\times\mathcal{W}_{x_{i}y_{0}}\times\mathcal{G}_{y_{0}\ldots y_{j}}

for
x=0,\ldots x_{i}
in
\mathcal{F}
and
y_{0},\ldots,y_{j}=y
in
\mathcal{G}
.

A
bordism

\mathcal{R}
between two such morphisms
\mathcal{W}
and
\mathcal{V}
consists of compact smooth manifolds with faces
\mathcal{R}_{xy}
(of dimension
|x|-|y|
+ 1) for
x
in
\mathcal{F}
and
y
in
\mathcal{G}
, along with maps

c=c^{\mathcal{R}}:\mathcal{W}_{xy},\mathcal{V}_{xy}\rightarrow\mathcal{R}_{xy}

and

c=c^{\mathcal{R}}:\mathcal{F}_{xz}\times\mathcal{R}_{zy}\rightarrow\mathcal{R}_{xy}

and

c=c^{\mathcal{R}}:\mathcal{R}_{xz}\times\mathcal{G}_{zy}\rightarrow\mathcal{R}_{xy}

compatible with
c^{\mathcal{F}}
,
c^{\mathcal{G}}
,
c^{\mathcal{W}}
,
c^{\mathcal{V}}
(meaning that similar diagrams to those above over each codimension two face commute), such that the images of the faces in the domains defines a system of faces for
\mathcal{R}_{xy}
(after deleting repeats). In other words, there is a system of faces for
\mathcal{R}_{xy}
given by the following:

\mathcal{W}_{x_{0}\ldots x_{i};y_{0}\ldots y_{j}}

and

\mathcal{V}_{x_{0}\ldots x_{i};y_{0}\ldots y_{j}}

and

\mathcal{R}_{x_{0}\ldots x_{i};y_{0}\ldots y_{j}}

for
x_{0}=x,\ldots,x_{i}
in
\mathcal{F}
and
y_{0},\ldots,y_{j}
in
\mathcal{G}
. We write
\mathcal{W}_{x_{0}\ldots x_{i};y_{0}\ldots y_{j}}
as shorthand for
\mathcal{F}_{x_{0}\ldots x_{i}}\times\mathcal{W}_{x_{i}y_{0}}\times\mathcal{G}_{y_{0}\ldots y_{j}}
, and similarly for
\mathcal{V}
,
\mathcal{R}
and any similar construction we encounter later.

A
system of collars
on a morphism
\mathcal{W}
is a system of faces on
\mathcal{F}
,
\mathcal{G}
and all
\mathcal{W}_{xy}
, which are compatible with the product systems of collars on all faces. A
system of collars
on a bordism
\mathcal{R}
between
\mathcal{W}
and
\mathcal{V}
is a system of collars on
\mathcal{F}
,
\mathcal{G}
,
\mathcal{W}
,
\mathcal{V}
and all
\mathcal{R}_{xy}
, which are compatible with the product system of collars on all faces.

Definition 49
.

A morphism from
\ast[i]
to
\mathcal{F}
is called a (right)
flow module
over
\mathcal{F}
of
degree

i
. Similarly, a morphism from
\mathcal{F}
to
\ast[j]
is a
left flow module
of degree
j
.

{rmk}

Right (respectively left) flow modules over
\mathcal{F}
are in particular right (left) modules over
\mathcal{F}
(viewed as a (non-unital) topological category). Similarly a morphism
\mathcal{W}:\mathcal{F}\to\mathcal{G}
in particular forms a bimodule over these two topological categories. Similarly a bilinear map (see Section
5.1
) of flow categories forms a multimodule over the relevant flow categories.

It follows from Lemma
35
that

Lemma 50
.

Every morphism
\mathcal{W}:\mathcal{F}\rightarrow\mathcal{G}
of flow categories admits a system of collars, and if
\mathcal{F}
and/or
\mathcal{G}
were already equipped with systems of collars, this can be chosen to be compatible with those.

Every bordism
\mathcal{R}
between morphisms
\mathcal{W},\mathcal{V}:\mathcal{F}\rightarrow\mathcal{G}
admits a system of collars, and if some of
\mathcal{F}
,
\mathcal{G}
,
\mathcal{W}
and
\mathcal{V}
are already equipped with systems of collars (compatibly with each other), this can be chosen to be compatible with these.

These are all unique up to isotopy.

Given this, we will often choose systems of collars on morphisms or homotopies and denote them
\mathcal{C}
, without explicitly stating that we do so. If we have already chosen systems of collars on the domain or target flow categories or morphisms, we choose these systems of collars to extend them. We will similarly implicitly do this on future manifolds we counter.

{ex}

Setting
\mathcal{W}_{xy}=\emptyset
for all
x\in\mathcal{F}
,
y\in\mathcal{G}
defines a map of flow categories
\mathcal{F}\rightarrow\mathcal{G}
, which we call the
empty morphism
.

If
\mathcal{W},\mathcal{V}:\mathcal{F}\rightarrow\mathcal{G}
are morphisms, setting

\left(\mathcal{W}\sqcup\mathcal{V}\right)_{xy}:=\mathcal{W}_{xy}\sqcup\mathcal{V}_{xy}

defines a morphism
\mathcal{W}\sqcup\mathcal{V}:\mathcal{F}\rightarrow\mathcal{G}
.

{ex}

Let
\mathcal{W}:\mathcal{F}\rightarrow\mathcal{G}
be a morphism of flow categories. There is a flow category
\Cone(\mathcal{W})
with objects
\mathcal{F}[1]\sqcup\mathcal{G}
, with morphisms given by

\Cone(\mathcal{W})_{xy}:=\begin{cases}\mathcal{F}_{xy}&\,\mathrm{if}\,x,y\in\mathcal{F}\\
\mathcal{G}_{xy}&\,\mathrm{if}\,x,y\in\mathcal{G}\\
\mathcal{W}_{xy}&\,\mathrm{if}\,x\in\mathcal{F},\,y\in\mathcal{G}\\
\emptyset&\,\mathrm{if}\,x\in\mathcal{G},\,y\in\mathcal{F}\end{cases}

Then
\Cone(\mathcal{W})
contains
\mathcal{F}[1]
and
\mathcal{G}
as full subcategories.

Definition 51
.

Let
\mathcal{F}
and
\mathcal{G}
be flow categories. Define
[\mathcal{F},\mathcal{G}]
to be the set of flow morphisms
\mathcal{F}\rightarrow\mathcal{G}
, modulo the equivalence relation generated by

1.

\mathcal{W}\sim\mathcal{V}
if
\mathcal{W}
and
\mathcal{V}
are bordant.

2.

\mathcal{W}\sim\mathcal{V}
if
\mathcal{W}
and
\mathcal{V}
are diffeomorphic (meaning there are diffeomorphisms
\mathcal{W}_{xy}\simeq\mathcal{V}_{xy}
for all
x
in
\mathcal{F}
and
y
in
\mathcal{G}
, compatible with all concatenation maps).

This is a commutative monoid under disjoint union, with unit given by the empty morphism.

Lemma 52
.

[\mathcal{F},\mathcal{G}]
contains inverses, and hence is a group.

Proof 4.2
.

Let
\mathcal{W}:\mathcal{F}\to\mathcal{G}
be a morphism. Then letting
\mathcal{R}_{xy}=\mathcal{W}_{xy}\times[0,1]
for
x
in
\mathcal{F}
and
y
in
\mathcal{G}
provides a nullbordism of
\mathcal{W}\sqcup\mathcal{W}
; this implies that
\mathcal{W}
is its own inverse.

Example 4.3
.

A morphism
*[i]\to*
consists of a closed
i
-manifold; a bordism between such morphisms consists of a bordism between the relevant closed manifolds in the usual sense. From this, we see that

[*[i],*]=\Omega_{i}(\ast)=\Omega_{i}

reconstructs the usual (unoriented) bordism groups of a point
\ast
.

Given morphisms of flow categories
\mathcal{W}:\mathcal{F}\rightarrow\mathcal{G}
and
\mathcal{V}:\mathcal{G}\rightarrow\mathcal{H}
, we construct their composition as follows.

We first define (non-compact) manifolds with faces
\tilde{\mathcal{Q}}_{xz}
for all
x\in\mathcal{F}
,
z\in\mathcal{H}
, by

\tilde{\mathcal{Q}}_{xz}=\tilde{\mathcal{Q}}_{xz}(\mathcal{W},\mathcal{V}):=\left(\bigsqcup_{y\in\mathcal{G}}\mathcal{W}_{xy}\times\mathcal{V}_{yz}\times[0,\varepsilon)\right)/\sim

(5)

where
\sim
is the equivalence relation such that for all
a\in\mathcal{W}_{xy}
,
b\in\mathcal{G}_{yy^{\prime}}
,
c\in\mathcal{V}_{y^{\prime}z}
,
(u,v)\in[0,\varepsilon)^{2}_{u,v}
,

(a,\mathcal{C}(b,c,u),v)\sim(\mathcal{C}(a,b,v),c,u)

(6)

As a topological space, we can write this as the coequaliser of the two maps above:

\bigsqcup_{y,y^{\prime}\in\mathcal{G}}\mathcal{W}_{xy}\times\mathcal{G}_{yy^{\prime}}\times\mathcal{V}_{yz}\times[0,\varepsilon)^{2}\rightrightarrows\bigsqcup_{y\in\mathcal{G}}\mathcal{W}_{xy}\times\mathcal{V}_{yz}\times[0,\varepsilon)

By Lemma
37
,
\tilde{\mathcal{Q}}_{xz}
is a manifold with corners. There are natural embeddings into it from the following manifolds with faces.

\mathcal{W}_{x_{0}\ldots x_{i};y_{0}}\times\mathcal{G}_{y_{0}\ldots y_{j}}\times\mathcal{V}_{y_{j};z_{0}\ldots z_{k}}\times\{0\}

and

\mathcal{F}_{x_{0}\ldots x_{i}}\times\tilde{\mathcal{Q}}_{x_{i}z_{0}}\times\mathcal{H}_{z_{0}\ldots z_{j}}

for
x_{0}=x,\ldots,x_{i}
in
\mathcal{F}
,
y_{0},\ldots,y_{j}
in
\mathcal{G}
and
z_{0},\ldots,z_{k}=z
in
\mathcal{H}
.

Lemma 53
.

These form a system of faces for
\tilde{\mathcal{Q}}_{xz}
. In particular, it is a manifold with faces.

Proof 4.4
.

This follows from Lemmas
37
and
38
; we spell this out in this case. We use
I=ob\mathcal{G}
,
J=ob\mathcal{F}\sqcup ob\mathcal{H}
, set
M_{z}=\mathcal{W}_{xz}\times\mathcal{V}_{yz}
,
N_{yy^{\prime}}=\mathcal{W}_{xy}\times\mathcal{G}_{yy^{\prime}}\times\mathcal{V}_{y^{\prime}z}
,
i_{yy^{\prime}}:=y
and
j_{yy^{\prime}}:=y^{\prime}
. We take each
\alpha_{yy^{\prime}},\beta_{yy^{\prime}}
to be given by
c
.

For
y^{\prime}\in I\setminus\{y\}
, we let
\lambda_{y}(y^{\prime})
be
\mathcal{W}_{xy}\times\mathcal{G}_{yy^{\prime}}\times\mathcal{V}_{y^{\prime}z}
(swapping
y
and
y^{\prime}
in this expression if
|y^{\prime}|>|y|
), and for
x^{\prime}\in\mathcal{F}
and
z^{\prime}\in\mathcal{H}
, we set
\lambda(x^{\prime})
to be
\mathcal{W}_{xx^{\prime};y}\times\mathcal{V}_{yz}
and
\lambda_{y}(z^{\prime})
to be
\mathcal{W}_{xy}\times\mathcal{V}_{y;z^{\prime}z}
; we similarly set
\lambda_{yy^{\prime}}(y^{\prime\prime})
to be
\mathcal{W}_{xy}\times\mathcal{G}_{yy^{\prime}}\times\mathcal{G}_{y^{\prime}y^{\prime\prime}}\times\mathcal{V}_{y^{\prime\prime}z}
(swapping the order of the elements of
\mathcal{G}
if necessary), and setting
\lambda_{yy^{\prime}}(x^{\prime})
to be
\mathcal{W}_{xx^{\prime};y}\times\mathcal{G}_{yy^{\prime}}\times\mathcal{V}_{yz}
and
\lambda_{yy^{\prime}}(z^{\prime})
to be
\mathcal{W}_{xy}\times\mathcal{G}_{yy^{\prime}}\times\mathcal{V}_{y^{\prime};z^{\prime}z}
.

We now choose framed functions
(f_{xz}:\tilde{\mathcal{Q}}_{xz}\rightarrow\mathbb{R},s_{xz})

such that
(f_{xz},s_{xz})
restricts to
(f_{x^{\prime}z},s_{x^{\prime}z})
and
(f_{xz^{\prime}},s_{xz^{\prime}})
on the boundary faces
\tilde{\mathcal{Q}}_{xx^{\prime};z}
and
\tilde{\mathcal{Q}}_{x;z^{\prime}z}
respectively. This can be done, by Lemma
41
. Note that we won’t use the sections
s_{xz}
until we deal with framings in Section
4.3
.

Let
\mathcal{Q}_{xz}^{\leq 1}=f_{xz}^{-1}(-\infty,1]
and
\mathcal{U}_{xz}=f_{xz}^{-1}\{1\}
. Noting that
\mathcal{U}_{xz}
is always disjoint from
\mathcal{W}_{xy}\times\mathcal{V}_{yz}
for all
y
, Lemma
40
implies that
\mathcal{Q}_{xz}^{\leq 1}
is a compact manifold with faces, with a system of faces given by

\mathcal{W}_{x_{0}\ldots x_{i};y_{0}}\times\mathcal{G}_{y_{0}\ldots y_{j}}\times\mathcal{V}_{y_{j};z_{0}\ldots z_{k}}

and

\mathcal{F}_{x_{0}\ldots x_{i}}\times\mathcal{Q}^{\leq 1}_{x_{i}z_{0}}\times\mathcal{H}_{z_{0}\ldots z_{j}}

and

\mathcal{F}_{x_{0}\ldots x_{i}}\times\mathcal{U}_{x_{i}z_{0}}\times\mathcal{H}_{z_{0}\ldots z_{j}}

for
x_{0}=x,\ldots,x_{i}
in
\mathcal{F}
,
y_{0},\ldots,y_{j}
in
\mathcal{G}
and
z_{0},\ldots,z_{k}=z
in
\mathcal{H}
.

Lemma
40
also implies that
\mathcal{U}_{xz}
is a manifold with faces, with a system of faces given by

\mathcal{F}_{x_{0}\ldots x_{i}}\times\mathcal{U}_{x_{i}z_{0}}\times\mathcal{H}_{z_{0}\ldots z_{j}}

for
x_{0}=x,\ldots,x_{i}
in
\mathcal{F}
and
z_{0},\ldots,z_{j}=z
in
\mathcal{H}
.
This implies the collection
\mathcal{U}_{xz}
form a morphism of flow categories
\mathcal{U}:\mathcal{F}\rightarrow\mathcal{H}
.

Definition 54
.

We define the
composition
of
\mathcal{W}
and
\mathcal{V}
to be
\mathcal{U}
.

Lemma 55
.

The equivalence class of
\mathcal{U}
in
[\mathcal{F},\mathcal{H}]
is independent of the intermediate choices.

Proof 4.5
.

The choices made in constructing
\mathcal{U}
were the choice of framed functions
(f_{xz},s_{xz})
.

Let
1\geq q>p>0
be regular values of all the
f_{xz}
, and let
\mathcal{U}^{f,p}_{xz}:=f^{-1}_{xz}\{p\}
and
\mathcal{U}^{f,q}_{xz}:=f^{-1}_{xz}\{q\}
.

Let
\mathcal{R}_{xz}=f_{xz}^{-1}[p,q]
. These form a bordism
\mathcal{R}
between
\mathcal{U}^{f,p}
and
\mathcal{U}^{f,q}
. In particular,
\mathcal{U}^{f,p}
and
\mathcal{U}^{f,q}
are both (bordant to) allowable constructions of representatives of
\mathcal{V}\circ\mathcal{W}
.

Suppose we are given two choices of framed functions: say
(f_{xz},s_{xz})
and
(f^{\prime}_{xz},s^{\prime}_{xz})
. Let
1>p>0
be a regular value of all
f_{xz}
, and let
1\geq q>p
be a regular value of all
f^{\prime}_{xz}
close enough to 1 such that
f^{\prime}_{xz}(f_{xz}^{-1}(-\infty,\mu])<\mu
for all
x,z
, where
\mu:=\frac{p+q}{2}
. We choose smooth functions
\alpha,\beta:\mathbb{R}\rightarrow[0,1]
such that

\alpha(t)=\begin{cases}1&\,\mathrm{if}\,t\leq p\\
0&\,\mathrm{if}\,\mu\leq t\end{cases}

and

\beta(t)=\begin{cases}0&\,\mathrm{if}\,t\leq\mu\\
1&\,\mathrm{if}\,q\leq t\end{cases}

We then define
g_{xz}:\tilde{\mathcal{Q}}_{xz}\rightarrow\mathbb{R}
:

g_{xz}(r):=\begin{cases}f_{xz}(r)&\,\mathrm{if}\,f_{xz}(r)\leq p\\
\alpha(f_{xz}(r))f_{xz}(r)+(1-\alpha(f_{xz}(r)))\left(\mu\right)&\,\mathrm{if}\,p\leq f_{xz}(r)\leq\mu\\
\mu&\,\mathrm{if}\,\mu\leq f_{xz}(r)\,\mathrm{and}\,f^{\prime}_{xz}(r)\leq\mu\\
\beta(f^{\prime}_{xz}(r))f^{\prime}_{xz}(r)+(1-\beta(f^{\prime}_{xz}(r)))\left(\mu\right)&\,\mathrm{if}\,\mu\leq f^{\prime}_{xz}(r)\leq q\\
f^{\prime}_{xz}(r)&\,\mathrm{if}\,q\leq f^{\prime}_{xz}(r)\end{cases}

Then taking
\mathcal{R}_{xz}:=g_{xz}^{-1}[p,q]
forms a bordism
\mathcal{R}
between
\mathcal{U}^{f,p}
and
\mathcal{U}^{f^{\prime},q}
.

Lemma 56
.

Composition is compatible with the equivalence relation defined in Definition
51
, and induces a well-defined bilinear map

\circ:[\mathcal{G},\mathcal{H}]\otimes[\mathcal{F},\mathcal{G}]\rightarrow[\mathcal{F},\mathcal{H}].

Proof 4.6
.

Let
\mathcal{W},\mathcal{W}^{\prime}:\mathcal{F}\rightarrow\mathcal{G}
and
\mathcal{V},\mathcal{V}^{\prime}:\mathcal{G}\rightarrow\mathcal{H}
be morphisms. Clearly if
\mathcal{W}
and
\mathcal{W}^{\prime}
are diffeomorphic and
\mathcal{V}
and
\mathcal{V}^{\prime}
are diffeomorphic then
\mathcal{V}\circ\mathcal{W}
and
\mathcal{V}^{\prime}\circ\mathcal{W}^{\prime}
are diffeomorphic (note all
\tilde{\mathcal{Q}}(\mathcal{W},\mathcal{V})_{xy}
and
\tilde{\mathcal{Q}}({\mathcal{W}^{\prime},\mathcal{V}^{\prime}})
are diffeomorphic, and we can pull back framed functions along such a diffeomorphism).

Let
\mathcal{R}
be a bordism between
\mathcal{W}
and
\mathcal{W}^{\prime}
. We construct a bordism
\mathcal{S}
between
\mathcal{V}\circ\mathcal{W}
and
\mathcal{V}\circ\mathcal{W}^{\prime}
as follows. The strategy is to perform the same construction as that of
\mathcal{V}\circ\mathcal{W}
, but with
\mathcal{W}
replaced with
\mathcal{R}
.

First define

\tilde{\mathcal{S}}_{xz}=\tilde{\mathcal{S}}_{xz}(\mathcal{R},\mathcal{V})=\left(\bigsqcup_{y\in\mathcal{G}}\mathcal{R}_{xy}\times\mathcal{V}_{yz}\times[0,\varepsilon)\right)/\sim

where
\sim
is defined the same as in the definition of
\tilde{\mathcal{Q}}
- more explicitly, we say that for all
a\in\mathcal{R}_{xy}
,
b\in\mathcal{G}_{yy^{\prime}}
,
c\in\mathcal{V}_{y^{\prime}z}
,
(u,v)\in[0,\varepsilon)^{2}
,

(a,\mathcal{C}(b,c,u),v)\sim(\mathcal{C}(a,b,v),c,u).

Lemmas
37
and
38
show that each
\tilde{\mathcal{S}}_{xz}
is a manifold with faces, with a system of faces given by

\mathcal{R}_{x_{0}\ldots x_{i};y_{0}}\times\mathcal{G}_{y_{0}\ldots y_{j}}\times\mathcal{V}_{y_{j};z_{0}\ldots z_{k}}\times\{0\}

and

\mathcal{W}_{x_{0}\ldots x_{i};y_{0}}\times\mathcal{G}_{y_{0}\ldots y_{j}}\times\mathcal{V}_{y_{j};z_{0}\ldots z_{k}}\times\{0\}

and

\mathcal{W}^{\prime}_{x_{0}\ldots x_{i};y_{0}}\times\mathcal{G}_{y_{0}\ldots y_{j}}\times\mathcal{V}_{y_{j};z_{0}\ldots z_{k}}\times\{0\}

and

\mathcal{F}_{x_{0}\ldots x_{i}}\times\tilde{\mathcal{Q}}_{x_{i}z_{0}}\times\mathcal{H}_{z_{0}\ldots z_{j}}

and

\mathcal{F}_{x_{0}\ldots x_{i}}\times\tilde{\mathcal{Q}}^{\prime}_{x_{i}z_{0}}\times\mathcal{H}_{z_{0}\ldots z_{j}}

and

\mathcal{F}_{x_{0}\ldots x_{i}}\times\tilde{\mathcal{S}}_{x_{i}z_{0}}\times\mathcal{H}_{z_{0}\ldots z_{j}}

for
x_{0}=x,\ldots,x_{i}
in
\mathcal{F}
,
y_{0},\ldots,y_{j}
in
\mathcal{G}
and
z_{0},\ldots,z_{k}=z
in
\mathcal{H}
, and where
\tilde{Q}:=\tilde{Q}(\mathcal{W},\mathcal{V})
and
\tilde{Q}^{\prime}:=\tilde{Q}(\mathcal{W}^{\prime},\mathcal{V})
.

Pick framed functions
(f_{xz},s_{xz})
on all
\tilde{\mathcal{Q}}_{xz},\tilde{\mathcal{Q}}^{\prime}_{xz}
and
\tilde{\mathcal{S}}_{xz}
, restricting to each other on overlaps.

Let
\mathcal{S}_{xz}^{\leq 1}=f_{xz}^{-1}(-\infty,1]
. Similarly to before, Lemma
40
implies that
\mathcal{S}_{xz}^{\leq 1}
is a compact manifold with faces, with a system of faces given by the above list with all
\tilde{\mathcal{S}}
s replaces with
\mathcal{S}^{\leq 1}
s, along with

\mathcal{F}_{x_{0}\ldots x_{i}}\times\mathcal{T}_{x_{i}z_{0}}\times\mathcal{H}_{z_{0}\ldots z_{j}}

where
\mathcal{T}_{xz}:=f_{xz}^{-1}\{1\}
.
\mathcal{T}_{xy}
is a compact manifold with faces, with a system of faces given by

\mathcal{F}_{x_{0}\ldots x_{i}}\times\mathcal{U}^{f,p}_{x_{i}z_{0}}\times\mathcal{H}_{z_{0}\ldots z_{j}}

and

\mathcal{F}_{x_{0}\ldots x_{i}}\times\mathcal{U}^{\prime f,p}_{x_{i}z_{0}}\times\mathcal{H}_{z_{0}\ldots z_{j}}

and

\mathcal{F}_{x_{0}\ldots x_{i}}\times\mathcal{T}_{x_{i}z_{0}}\times\mathcal{H}_{z_{0}\ldots z_{j}}

for
x_{0}=x,\ldots,x_{i}
in
\mathcal{F}
and
z_{0},\ldots,z_{k}=z
in
\mathcal{H}
, and where
\mathcal{U}:=\mathcal{U}^{f,p}(\mathcal{W},\mathcal{V})
and
\mathcal{U}^{\prime}:=\mathcal{U}^{f,p}(\mathcal{W}^{\prime},\mathcal{V})
are representatives of
\mathcal{V}\circ\mathcal{W}
and
\mathcal{V}\circ\mathcal{W}^{\prime}
respectively. Therefore
\mathcal{T}
is a bordism between
\mathcal{U}^{f,p}
and
\mathcal{U}^{\prime f,p}
, as required.

The same argument shows that if
\mathcal{V}
and
\mathcal{V}^{\prime}
are bordant, so are
\mathcal{V}\circ\mathcal{W}
and
\mathcal{V}^{\prime}\circ\mathcal{W}
.

It is clear from construction that

(\mathcal{V}\sqcup\mathcal{V}^{\prime})\circ(\mathcal{W}\sqcup\mathcal{W}^{\prime})=(\mathcal{V}\circ\mathcal{W})\sqcup(\mathcal{V}\circ\mathcal{W}^{\prime})\sqcup(\mathcal{V}^{\prime}\circ\mathcal{W})\sqcup(\mathcal{V}^{\prime}\circ\mathcal{W}^{\prime})

which implies that composition induces a bilinear map of groups

\circ:[\mathcal{G},\mathcal{H}]\otimes[\mathcal{F},\mathcal{G}]\rightarrow[\mathcal{F},\mathcal{H}]

Lemma 57
.

The composition map is associative, i.e. the following diagram commutes:

\textstyle{\left[\mathcal{H},\mathcal{I}\right]\otimes\left[\mathcal{G},\mathcal{H}\right]\otimes\left[\mathcal{F},\mathcal{G}\right]\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\circ}
\scriptstyle{\circ}
\textstyle{\left[\mathcal{G},\mathcal{I}\right]\otimes\left[\mathcal{F},\mathcal{G}\right]\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\circ}
\textstyle{\left[\mathcal{H},\mathcal{I}\right]\otimes\left[\mathcal{F},\mathcal{H}\right]\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\circ}
\textstyle{\left[\mathcal{F},\mathcal{I}\right]}

(7)

Proof 4.7
.

Choose morphisms
\mathcal{W}:\mathcal{F}\to\mathcal{G}
,
\mathcal{V}:\mathcal{G}\to\mathcal{H}
and
\mathcal{X}:\mathcal{H}\to\mathcal{I}
.

Let
\tilde{\mathcal{Q}}_{xz}=\tilde{\mathcal{Q}}(\mathcal{W},\mathcal{V})_{xz}
and
\tilde{\mathcal{Q}}^{\prime}_{yw}=\tilde{\mathcal{Q}}_{yw}(\mathcal{V},\mathcal{X})
for all
x\in\mathcal{F}
,
y\in\mathcal{G}
,
z\in\mathcal{H}
and
w\in\mathcal{I}
.

Choose framed functions
(f_{xz},s_{xz})
on each
\tilde{\mathcal{Q}}_{xz}
and
(f^{\prime}_{yw},s^{\prime}_{yw})
on each
\tilde{\mathcal{Q}}^{\prime}_{yw}
, suitably compatible on overlaps.

For
x\in\mathcal{F}
and
w\in\mathcal{I}
, define
\tilde{\mathcal{P}}_{xw}
by

\tilde{\mathcal{P}}_{xw}:=\left(\bigsqcup\limits_{z\in\mathcal{H}}\tilde{\mathcal{Q}}_{xz}\times\mathcal{X}_{zw}\times[0,\varepsilon)_{s}\right)/\sim

where
\sim
is defined so that
\tilde{\mathcal{P}}_{xw}
is the coequaliser of the following diagram:

\textstyle{\bigsqcup\limits_{z,z^{\prime}\in\mathcal{H}}\tilde{\mathcal{Q}}_{xz}\times\mathcal{H}_{zz^{\prime}}\times\mathcal{X}_{z^{\prime}w}\times[0,\varepsilon)^{2}_{u,v}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\bigsqcup\limits_{z\in\mathcal{H}}\tilde{\mathcal{Q}}_{xz}\times\mathcal{X}_{zw}\times[0,\varepsilon)_{s}}

where the two maps send
(a,b,c,u,v)
to
(a,\mathcal{C}(b,c,u),v)
and
(\mathcal{C}(a,b,v),c,u)
respectively.

Similarly we define
\tilde{\mathcal{P}}^{\prime}_{xw}
to be the coequaliser of the following diagram:

\textstyle{\bigsqcup\limits_{y,y^{\prime}\in\mathcal{G}}\mathcal{W}_{xy}\times\mathcal{G}_{yy^{\prime}}\times\tilde{\mathcal{Q}}^{\prime}_{y^{\prime}w}\times[0,\varepsilon)^{2}_{u,v}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\bigsqcup\limits_{y\in\mathcal{G}}\mathcal{W}_{xy}\times\tilde{\mathcal{Q}}^{\prime}_{yw}\times[0,\varepsilon)_{s}}

with the maps defined similarly.

Note
\tilde{\mathcal{P}}_{xw}
and
\tilde{\mathcal{P}}^{\prime}_{xw}
both have dimension
|x|-|w|+2
.

By Lemma
38
,
\tilde{\mathcal{P}}_{xw}
has a system of boundary faces given by
\tilde{\mathcal{P}}_{xx^{\prime};w}
,
\tilde{\mathcal{P}}_{x;w^{\prime}w}
and
\tilde{\mathcal{Q}}_{xz}\times\mathcal{X}_{zw}
, for
x^{\prime}\in\mathcal{F}
,
w^{\prime}\in\mathcal{I}
and
z\in\mathcal{H}
. Choose framed functions
(h_{xw},r_{xw})
on each
\tilde{\mathcal{P}}_{xw}
, compatibly on boundary faces.

Similarly,
\tilde{\mathcal{P}}^{\prime}_{xw}
has a system of boundary faces given by
\tilde{\mathcal{P}}^{\prime}_{xx^{\prime};w}
,
\tilde{\mathcal{P}}^{\prime}_{x;w^{\prime}w}
and
\mathcal{W}_{xy}\times\tilde{\mathcal{Q}}^{\prime}_{xw}
, for
x^{\prime}\in\mathcal{F}
,
w^{\prime}\in\mathcal{I}
and
y\in\mathcal{G}
. Choose framed functions
(h^{\prime}_{xw},r^{\prime}_{xw})
on each
\tilde{\mathcal{P}}^{\prime}_{xw}
, compatibly on boundary faces.

Let
\mathcal{M}_{xw}=h_{xw}^{-1}\{1\}
and
\mathcal{M}^{\prime}_{xw}=h^{\prime-1}_{xw}\{1\}
; then
\mathcal{M}
and
\mathcal{M}^{\prime}
form representatives of
\mathcal{X}\circ(\mathcal{V}\circ\mathcal{W})
and
(\mathcal{X}\circ\mathcal{V})\circ\mathcal{W}
respectively.

Now define
\tilde{\mathcal{Z}}_{xw}
to be the coequaliser of the following diagram:

\textstyle{\bigsqcup\limits_{\begin{subarray}{c}y\in\mathcal{G}\\
z\in\mathcal{H}\end{subarray}}\mathcal{W}_{xy}\times\mathcal{V}_{yz}\times\mathcal{X}_{zw}\times[0,\varepsilon)^{2}_{u,v}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\bigsqcup\limits_{y\in\mathcal{G}}\mathcal{W}_{xy}\times\tilde{\mathcal{Q}}_{yw}\times[0,\varepsilon)_{s}}
\textstyle{\bigsqcup\limits_{z\in\mathcal{H}}\tilde{\mathcal{Q}}_{xz}\times\mathcal{X}_{zw}\times[0,\varepsilon)_{s}}

where the maps are defined similarly to above. This has a system of boundary faces given by
\tilde{\mathcal{Z}}_{xx^{\prime};w}
,
\tilde{\mathcal{Z}}_{x;w^{\prime}w}
,
\tilde{\mathcal{Q}}_{xz}\times\mathcal{X}_{zw}\times\{0\}
,
\mathcal{W}_{xy}\times\tilde{\mathcal{Q}}_{yw}^{\prime}\times\{0\}
, for
x^{\prime}\in\mathcal{F}
,
w^{\prime}\in\mathcal{I}
,
y\in\mathcal{G}
and
z\in\mathcal{H}
. Choose framed functions
(g_{xw},t_{xw})
on the
\tilde{\mathcal{Z}}_{xw}
extending the
(f_{xz},s_{xz})
on the
\tilde{\mathcal{Q}}_{xz}
and the
(f^{\prime}_{yw},s^{\prime}_{yw})
on the
\tilde{\mathcal{Q}}^{\prime}_{xz}
respectively. Note that faces of
\tilde{\mathcal{Z}}_{xw}
of the form
\tilde{\mathcal{Q}}_{xz}\times\mathcal{X}_{zw}\times\{0\}
and
\mathcal{W}_{xy}\times\tilde{\mathcal{Q}}_{yw}^{\prime}\times\{0\}
can only overlap in compact codimension 2 faces, so when considering
g_{xw}^{-1}\{1\}
the corresponding faces won’t overlap at all, since
g_{xw}
takes negative values on compact faces.

Let
\mathcal{Y}_{xw}=g_{xw}^{-1}\{1\}
; this is a compact manifold with faces of dimension
|x|-|w|+1
. Let
\mathcal{U}_{xz}=f^{-1}_{xz}\{1\}
and
\mathcal{U}^{\prime}_{yw}=f^{\prime-1}_{yw}\{1\}
; then
\mathcal{U}
and
\mathcal{U}^{\prime}
are representatives of
\mathcal{V}\circ\mathcal{W}
and
\mathcal{X}\circ\mathcal{V}
respectively.

\mathcal{Y}_{xw}
has a system of boundary faces given by
\mathcal{Y}_{xx^{\prime};w}
,
\mathcal{Y}_{x;w^{\prime}w}
,
\mathcal{U}_{xz}\times\mathcal{X}_{zw}
and
\mathcal{W}_{xy}\times\mathcal{U}^{\prime}_{yw}
, for
x^{\prime}\in\mathcal{F}
,
w^{\prime}\in\mathcal{I}
,
y\in\mathcal{G}
and
z\in\mathcal{H}
. As noted before, the last two types of face here do not intersect each other:
\mathcal{U}_{xz}\times\mathcal{X}_{zw}
and
\mathcal{W}_{xy}\times\mathcal{U}^{\prime}_{yw}
are disjoint, for all
y\in\mathcal{G}
and
z\in\mathcal{H}
.

By the collar neighbourhood theorem, there are disjoint embeddings
\tilde{\mathcal{P}}_{xw},\tilde{\mathcal{P}}^{\prime}_{xw}\hookrightarrow\mathcal{Y}_{xw}
onto neighbourhoods of
\cup_{z\in\mathcal{H}}\mathcal{U}_{xz}\times\mathcal{X}_{zw}
and
\cup_{y\in\mathcal{G}}\mathcal{W}_{xy}\times\mathcal{U}^{\prime}_{yw}
respectively; furthermore these can be chosen to be compatible with each other under the inclusions
c
.

We now remove (the images under the above embeddings) of each
h^{-1}_{xw}(-\infty,1)\subseteq\tilde{\mathcal{P}}_{xw}
and
h^{\prime-1}_{xw}(-\infty,1)\subseteq\tilde{\mathcal{P}}^{\prime}_{xw}
from each
\mathcal{Y}_{xw}
to get a manifold
\mathcal{L}_{xw}
; explicitly

\mathcal{L}_{xw}=\mathcal{Y}_{xw}\setminus\left(h_{xw}^{-1}(-\infty,1)\sqcup h^{\prime-1}_{xw}(-\infty,1)\right)

Now each
\mathcal{L}_{xw}
has a system of boundary faces given by
\mathcal{L}_{xx^{\prime};w}
,
\mathcal{L}_{x;w^{\prime}w}
,
\mathcal{M}_{xw}
and
\mathcal{M}^{\prime}_{xw}
; therefore
\mathcal{L}
forms a bordism between
\mathcal{M}_{xw}
and
\mathcal{M}^{\prime}_{xw}
.

Lemma 58
.

Let
\mathcal{W}:\mathcal{F}\to\mathcal{G}
,
\mathcal{V}:\mathcal{G}\to\mathcal{H}
and
\mathcal{T}:\mathcal{F}\to\mathcal{H}
be morphisms. Suppose we are given compact manifolds with faces
\mathcal{R}_{xz}
for all
x
in
\mathcal{F}
and
z
in
\mathcal{H}
, of dimension
|x|-|z|
, with systems of faces given by

\mathcal{W}_{x_{0}\ldots x_{i};y_{0}}\times\mathcal{G}_{y_{0}\ldots y_{j}}\times\mathcal{H}_{y_{j};z_{0}\ldots z_{k}}

and

\mathcal{T}_{x_{0}\ldots x_{i};z_{0}\ldots z_{k}}

and

\mathcal{R}_{x_{0}\ldots x_{i};z_{0}\ldots z_{k}}

for
x_{0}=x,\ldots,x_{i}
in
\mathcal{F}
,
y_{0},\ldots,y_{j}
in
\mathcal{G}
and
z_{0},\ldots,z_{k}=z
in
\mathcal{H}
.

Then
\mathcal{T}
is a representative of
\mathcal{V}\circ\mathcal{W}
.

Proof 4.8
.

Compatible collar neighbourhoods define compatible embeddings

\tilde{\mathcal{Q}}_{xz}(\mathcal{W},\mathcal{V})\hookrightarrow\mathcal{R}_{xz}

for all
x
in
\mathcal{F}
and
z
in
\mathcal{H}
.
We choose framed functions
(f_{xz},s_{xz})
on each
\tilde{\mathcal{Q}}_{xz}(\mathcal{W},\mathcal{V})
, compatible with each other, along with compatible extensions
g_{xz}
to functions
\mathcal{R}_{xz}\to\mathbb{R}
which are
>1
outside each
\tilde{\mathcal{Q}}_{xz}(\mathcal{W},\mathcal{V})
.

Then the
g_{xz}^{-1}[p,\infty)
forms a bordism of morphisms between
\mathcal{V}\circ\mathcal{W}
and
\mathcal{T}
.

We have constructed (and showed well-definedness of):

Definition 59
.

The
(homotopy) category of flow categories
,
\Flow
, has objects flow categories, morphisms
[\cdot,\cdot]
, and composition given as above. This is a (non-unital) category enriched in abelian groups.

By Example
4.3
,
\Flow
is in fact enriched in modules over
\Omega_{*}
.

{rmk}

We do not require
\Flow
to be unital and so we do not prove that it is, but we expect it to be.

Conjecture 60

(Abouzaid-Blumberg)
.

\Flow
is equivalent to the homotopy category of perfect
MO
-modules.

4.2
Abstract index bundles

Before defining stable framings on flow categories, we must first introduce abstract index bundles. In this subsection, we associate abstract index bundles to all of the manifolds we encountered in the previous section, along with isomorphisms between them compatible with the inclusions of boundary strata. These will be required to satisfy appropriate associativity conditions.

Definition 61
.

Let
\mathcal{M}_{xy}
be a manifold arising in the previous section (for example,
\mathcal{M}
could be a flow category and
x,y
objects in it, or
\mathcal{M}:\mathcal{F}\to\mathcal{G}
could be a morphism, with
x\in\mathcal{F}
or
y\in\mathcal{G}
). Its
abstract index bundle
is the vector bundle:

I^{\mathcal{M}}_{xy}:=T\mathcal{M}_{xy}\oplus\mathbb{R}\tau_{y}^{\mathcal{M}}

over
\mathcal{M}
. Here
\tau^{\mathcal{M}}_{y}
is a formal generator of the real line
\mathbb{R}\tau^{\mathcal{M}}_{y}
.

When it is clear, we will sometimes drop the superscript
\mathcal{M}
from the notation.

We call a boundary face of
\mathcal{M}_{xy}
equipped with a product decomposition a
broken boundary face
(for example this includes all boundary faces of all manifolds associated to a flow category or a morphism of flow categories). However not all boundary faces of the previous section are of this form (for example, if
\mathcal{R}
is a bordism between two morphisms
\mathcal{W},\mathcal{W}^{\prime}:\mathcal{F}\to\mathcal{G}
, then the boundary faces
\mathcal{W}_{xy}
and
\mathcal{W}^{\prime}_{xy}
of
\mathcal{R}_{xy}
aren’t broken boundary faces). The other boundary faces we label as either
incoming
or
outgoing
boundary faces.

{rmk}

Note that the notions of broken/unbroken and incoming/outgoing boundary faces are entirely a case of labelling and are not an intrinsic invariant of the manifolds in question.

Note that in all cases, if two boundary faces intersect, at least one of them is broken.

There were only two families of unbroken boundary faces we encountered in Section
4.1
. The first was in the boundary faces of a bordism
\mathcal{R}
between morphisms
\mathcal{W}
and
\mathcal{W}^{\prime}
; we declare the boundary faces
\mathcal{W}_{xy}
of
\mathcal{R}_{xy}
to be incoming and the boundary faces
\mathcal{W}^{\prime}_{xy}
of
\mathcal{R}_{xy}
to be outgoing. The second was in the boundary faces of the glued manifold
\tilde{\mathcal{S}}(\mathcal{R},\mathcal{V})_{xz}
, considered in Lemma
56
for
\mathcal{V}:\mathcal{G}\to\mathcal{H}
another morphism; in this case, the boundary faces
\tilde{\mathcal{Q}}(\mathcal{W},\mathcal{V})_{xz}
are declared to be incoming and the boundary faces
\tilde{\mathcal{Q}}(\mathcal{W}^{\prime},\mathcal{V})_{xz}
are declared to be outgoing.

Definition 62
.

Let
\mathcal{A}_{xy}
,
\mathcal{B}_{yz}
and
\mathcal{M}_{xz}
be manifolds arising in the previous section along with an inclusion of a boundary face
c:\mathcal{A}_{xy}\times\mathcal{B}_{yz}\to\mathcal{M}_{xz}
(for example,
\mathcal{A}
,
\mathcal{B}
and
\mathcal{M}
could all be morphisms in some fixed flow category, with objects
x,y,z
, with
c
the composition map) (for the purposes of this definition,
x
,
y
and
z
are entirely decorative). Assume they are equipped with systems of collars with which
c
is compatible, along with compatible Riemannian metrics respecting the splittings on all collar neighbourhoods. Let
\nu_{y}^{\mathcal{A}\times\mathcal{B}}
be the inwards pointing normal along this boundary face which is orthogonal to it. We define an isomorphism of vector bundles

\psi=\psi_{xyz}^{\mathcal{M}}:I^{\mathcal{A}}_{xy}\oplus I^{\mathcal{B}}_{yz}\to I^{\mathcal{M}}_{xz}

over the boundary face
\mathcal{A}_{xy}\times\mathcal{B}_{yz}
, to send
\tau_{y}^{\mathcal{A}}
to
\nu_{y}
and
\tau_{z}^{\mathcal{B}}
to
\tau_{z}^{\mathcal{M}}
, and to be given by
dc
on the other factors.

Now let
c:\mathcal{D}_{xy}\to\mathcal{M}_{xy}
be the inclusion of an unbroken boundary face. Again assume they are equipped with compatible systems of collars and Riemannian metrics, and let
\nu^{\mathcal{D}}
be the inwards pointing normal along this boundary face which is orthogonal to it. We define isomorphisms of vector bundles

\psi=\psi^{\mathcal{D}}_{xy}:I^{\mathcal{D}}_{xy}\oplus\mathbb{R}\sigma\to I^{\mathcal{M}}_{xy}

where
\sigma=\sigma^{\mathcal{D}}_{xy}
is a formal generator of the real line
\mathbb{R}\sigma
, to send
\sigma
to
+\nu^{\mathcal{D}}
if
\mathcal{D}
is an incoming boundary face, and
-\nu^{\mathcal{D}}
if
\mathcal{D}
is an outgoing boundary face, and given by
dc
on the other factors.
We call all of the above maps
\psi

abstract gluing isomorphisms
.

The subscripts are not a necessary part of this definition, but they are present in all cases of interest, so we include them for clarity.

Proposition 63
.

The
\psi
are associative over codimension two faces, in the following sense.

First we consider a codimension two face which is the intersection of two broken boundary faces. This means there are inclusions (all called
c
) of boundary faces
\mathcal{A}_{xy}\times\mathcal{B}_{yz}\to\mathcal{M}_{xz}
,
\mathcal{B}_{yz}\times\mathcal{C}_{zw}\to\mathcal{N}_{yw}
,
\mathcal{A}_{xy}\times\mathcal{N}_{yw}\to\mathcal{O}_{xw}
, and
\mathcal{M}_{xz}\times\mathcal{C}_{zw}\to\mathcal{O}_{xw}
, such that the following diagram commutes:

\textstyle{\mathcal{A}_{xy}\times\mathcal{B}_{yz}\times\mathcal{C}_{zw}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathcal{A}_{xy}\times\mathcal{N}_{yw}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathcal{M}_{xz}\times\mathcal{C}_{zw}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathcal{O}_{xw}}

Then the following diagram commutes:

\textstyle{I^{\mathcal{A}}_{xy}\oplus I^{\mathcal{B}}_{yz}\oplus I^{\mathcal{C}}_{zw}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\psi}
\scriptstyle{\psi}
\textstyle{I^{\mathcal{A}}_{xy}\oplus I^{\mathcal{N}}_{yw}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\psi}
\textstyle{I^{\mathcal{M}}_{xz}\oplus I^{\mathcal{C}}_{zw}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\psi}
\textstyle{I^{\mathcal{O}}_{xw}}

over
\mathcal{A}_{xy}\times\mathcal{B}_{yz}\times\mathcal{C}_{zw}
.

Secondly, we consider a codimension two face which is the intersection of one broken and one unbroken boundary face. Let
\mathcal{A}_{xy}\times\mathcal{B}_{yz}\to\mathcal{M}_{xz}
be an inclusion of a broken boundary face, and
\mathcal{D}_{xz}\to\mathcal{M}_{xz}
an unbroken boundary face. Assume that the codimension two face
\mathcal{D}_{xz}\cap(\mathcal{A}_{xy}\times\mathcal{B}_{yz})
is given by
\mathcal{A}_{xy}\times\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{yz}
, where
\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{yz}\to\mathcal{B}_{yz}
is an unbroken boundary face. Assume also that
\mathcal{D}
and
\mathcal{D}^{\prime}
are either both incoming or outgoing boundary faces. Then the following diagram commutes:

\textstyle{I^{\mathcal{A}}_{xy}\oplus I^{\mathcal{D}^{\prime}}_{yz}\oplus\mathbb{R}\sigma\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\psi}
\scriptstyle{\psi}
\textstyle{I^{\mathcal{A}}_{xy}\oplus I^{\mathcal{B}}_{yz}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\psi}
\textstyle{I^{\mathcal{D}}_{xz}\oplus\mathbb{R}\sigma\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\psi}
\textstyle{I^{\mathcal{M}}_{xz}}

There is a similar commutative diagram in the case that
\mathcal{D}_{xz}\cap(\mathcal{A}_{xy}\times\mathcal{B}_{yz})
is given by
\mathcal{A}_{xy}\times\mathcal{D}_{yz}^{\prime\prime}
, where
\mathcal{D}_{yz}^{\prime\prime}\to\mathcal{B}_{yz}
is an unbroken boundary face.

Proof 4.9
.

In the first case, both ways around the diagram send
\tau_{y}
to
\nu_{y}
,
\tau_{z}
to
\nu_{y}
and
\nu_{z}
to
\tau_{w}
, and are given by
dc
on the other factors.

In the second case, both ways around the diagram send
\tau_{y}
to
\nu_{y}
,
\tau_{z}
to
\tau_{z}
and
\sigma
to
\pm\nu^{\mathcal{D}}
, where the
\pm
depends only on whether
\mathcal{D}
and
\mathcal{D}^{\prime}
are incoming or outgoing boundary faces.

4.3
Stable framings

We now incorporate stable framings into the category of flow categories, to obtain a category of framed flow categories.

Definition 64
.

A
(stable) framing
on a flow category
\mathcal{F}
consists of stable vector spaces
V_{x}
for each
x\in\mathcal{F}
, along with stable isomorphisms

st=st^{\mathcal{F}}_{xx^{\prime}}:V_{x}\oplus I^{\mathcal{F}}_{xx^{\prime}}\to V_{x^{\prime}}

with stabilising bundle
\mathbb{T}_{xx^{\prime}}^{\mathcal{F}}
for all
x,x^{\prime}\in\mathcal{F}
, along with embeddings

\iota_{xx^{\prime}x^{\prime\prime}}^{\mathcal{F}}:\mathbb{T}_{xx^{\prime}}\oplus V_{x^{\prime}}^{-}\oplus\mathbb{T}_{x^{\prime}x^{\prime\prime}}\hookrightarrow\mathbb{T}_{xx^{\prime\prime}}

over each
\mathcal{F}_{xx^{\prime}}\times\mathcal{F}_{x^{\prime}x^{\prime\prime}}
, which are associative in the sense that the following diagram commutes:

\textstyle{\mathbb{T}_{xx^{\prime}}\oplus V^{-}_{x^{\prime}}\oplus\mathbb{T}_{x^{\prime}x^{\prime\prime}}\oplus V^{-}_{x^{\prime\prime}}\oplus\mathbb{T}_{x^{\prime\prime}x^{\prime\prime\prime}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathbb{T}_{xx^{\prime\prime}}\oplus V^{-}_{x^{\prime\prime}}\oplus\mathbb{T}_{x^{\prime\prime}x^{\prime\prime\prime}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathbb{T}_{xx^{\prime}}\oplus V^{-}_{x^{\prime}}\oplus\mathbb{T}_{x^{\prime}x^{\prime\prime\prime}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathbb{T}_{xx^{\prime\prime\prime}}}

(8)

For all
x,x^{\prime},x^{\prime\prime}\in\mathcal{F}
, consider the following diagram of stable isomorphisms:

\textstyle{V_{x}\oplus I^{\mathcal{F}}_{xx^{\prime}}\oplus I^{\mathcal{F}}_{x^{\prime}x^{\prime\prime}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{st^{\mathcal{F}}}
\scriptstyle{\psi}
\textstyle{V_{x^{\prime}}\oplus I^{\mathcal{F}}_{x^{\prime}x^{\prime\prime}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{st^{\mathcal{F}}}
\textstyle{V_{x}\oplus I^{\mathcal{F}}_{xx^{\prime\prime}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{st^{\mathcal{F}}}
\textstyle{V_{x^{\prime\prime}}}

(9)

Composition both ways round do not have the same stabilising bundle: the composition right then down has stabilising bundle
\mathbb{T}_{xx^{\prime}}\oplus V_{x^{\prime}}^{-}\oplus\mathbb{T}_{x^{\prime}x^{\prime\prime}}
whereas the composition down then right has stabilising bundle
\mathbb{T}_{xx^{\prime\prime}}
. However we are given an embedding
\mathbb{T}_{xx^{\prime}}\oplus V^{-}_{x^{\prime}}\oplus\mathbb{T}_{x^{\prime}x^{\prime\prime}}\to\mathbb{T}_{xx^{\prime}}
. We require that after extending the stable isomorphism given by composition right then down along this embedding, that this diagram commutes.

We will often drop the sub- and/or super-scripts from
st
,
\mathbb{T}
and
\iota
to avoid clutter, if unambiguous from context. Similarly, we will write, for example,
\mathbb{T}_{xx^{\prime}x^{\prime\prime}}
as shorthand for
\mathbb{T}_{xx^{\prime}}\oplus V^{-}_{x^{\prime}}\oplus\mathbb{T}_{x^{\prime}x^{\prime\prime}}
, when necessary providing superscripts to indicate which stabilising bundles this refers to.

Example 4.10
.

Given manifolds
\mathcal{A}_{xy}
and
\mathcal{B}_{yz}
, stable vector spaces
V_{x},V_{y},V_{z}
, integers
a,b\geq 0
, and stable isomorphisms
st^{\mathcal{A}}:V_{x}\oplus I^{\mathcal{A}}_{xy}\to\mathbb{R}^{a}\oplus V_{y}
and
st^{\mathcal{B}}:V_{y}\oplus I^{\mathcal{B}}_{yz}\to\mathbb{R}^{b}\oplus V_{z}
, there is an induced stable isomorphism

st^{\mathcal{B}}\circ st^{\mathcal{A}}:V_{x}\oplus I^{\mathcal{A}}_{xy}\oplus I^{\mathcal{B}}_{yz}\to\mathbb{R}^{a}\oplus\mathbb{R}^{b}\oplus V_{z}\to\mathbb{R}^{a+b}\oplus V_{z}

where the final map identifies
\mathbb{R}^{a}\oplus\mathbb{R}^{b}
with
\mathbb{R}^{a+b}
preserving the orders of all entries.

Similarly, if
\mathcal{A}
is an unbroken boundary face of
\mathcal{D}
and given a stable isomorphism

dt^{\mathcal{D}}_{xy}:V_{x}\oplus I^{\mathcal{D}}_{xy}\to\mathbb{R}^{a}\oplus V_{y}

then we may consider the stable isomorphism with stabilising bundle
\mathbb{T}^{\mathcal{A}}_{xy}\oplus\mathbb{R}\sigma_{xy}^{\mathcal{A}}
:

st^{\mathcal{A}}_{xy}:V_{x}\oplus I_{xy}^{\mathcal{A}}\to\mathbb{R}^{a}\oplus V_{y}

obtained from
\psi
and
st^{\mathcal{D}}
, sending
\sigma
to
\sigma
.

Note that in either case, we do not switch the order of the factors of
\mathbb{R}
.

Definition 65
.

Let
\mathcal{M}_{xz}
be a manifold with faces, equipped with a stable isomorphism with stabilising bundle
\mathbb{T}^{\mathcal{A}}_{xz}

st^{\mathcal{M}}_{xz}:V_{x}\oplus I^{\mathcal{M}}_{xz}\to\mathbb{R}^{m}\oplus V_{z}

Assume that for each broken boundary face
\mathcal{A}_{xy}\times\mathcal{B}_{yz}\to\mathcal{M}
, we are given stable isomorphisms with stabilising bundles
\mathbb{T}^{\mathcal{A}}_{xy}
and
\mathbb{T}^{\mathcal{B}}_{yz}
respectively

st^{\mathcal{A}}_{xy}:V_{x}\oplus I^{\mathcal{A}}_{xy}\to\mathbb{R}^{a}\oplus V_{y}

and

st^{\mathcal{B}}_{yz}:V_{y}\oplus I^{\mathcal{B}}_{yz}\to\mathbb{R}^{b}\oplus V_{z}

such that
a+b=m
, and also that for each unbroken boundary face
\mathcal{D}_{xz}\to\mathcal{M}_{xz}

\mathcal{A}_{xy}\times\mathcal{B}_{yz}
, and

\iota^{\mathbb{D}}_{xz}:\mathbb{T}_{xz}^{\mathcal{D}}\hookrightarrow\mathbb{T}_{xz}^{\mathcal{M}}

over each unbroken boundary face
\mathcal{D}_{xz}
.
We say that these form a
system of framings
for
\mathcal{M}_{xz}
if over each boundary face, these stable framings and embeddings are compatible with each other via the abstract gluing isomorphisms, in the following sense.

Consider the following diagrams: over each broken boundary face
\mathcal{A}_{xy}\times\mathcal{B}_{yz}\to\mathcal{M}_{xz}
:

\textstyle{V_{x}\oplus I^{\mathcal{A}}_{xy}\oplus I^{\mathcal{B}}_{yz}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}

\scriptstyle{st^{\mathcal{M}}}
\textstyle{\mathbb{R}^{m}\oplus V_{z}}

(
\star
)

and over each unbroken boundary face
\mathcal{D}_{xz}\to\mathcal{M}_{xz}
, viewing
\mathbb{R}\sigma^{\mathcal{D}}_{xz}=
the final copy of
\mathbb{R}
in
\mathbb{R}^{m}
as the stabilising bundle:

\textstyle{V_{x}\oplus I^{\mathcal{D}}_{xz}\oplus\mathbb{R}\sigma^{\mathcal{D}}_{xz}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{st^{\mathcal{D}}}
\scriptstyle{\psi}
\textstyle{V_{x}\oplus I^{\mathcal{M}}_{xz}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{st^{\mathcal{M}}}
\textstyle{\mathbb{R}^{m}\oplus V_{z}}

We require that (

) and (

) both commute, after extending the stable isomorphism along the diagonal along the appropriate embedding
\iota
.

Example 4.11
.

Let
\mathcal{F}
be a framed flow category. Then each
st^{\mathcal{F}}_{xx^{\prime\prime}}
, along with the induced framings
st^{\mathcal{F}}_{xx^{\prime}}\circ st^{\mathcal{F}}_{x^{\prime}x^{\prime\prime}}
on each boundary face and the embeddings
\iota_{xx^{\prime}x^{\prime\prime}}
, form a system of framings for each
\mathcal{F}_{xx^{\prime\prime}}
.

Definition 66
.

We define framings for every type of object introduced in Section
4.1
.

1.

Let
\mathcal{W}:\mathcal{F}\to\mathcal{G}
be a morphism of flow categories, and assume that
\mathcal{F}
and
\mathcal{G}
are framed. A
framing
on
\mathcal{W}
consists of stable isomorphisms

st^{\mathcal{W}}_{xy}:V_{x}\oplus I^{\mathcal{W}}_{xy}\to\mathbb{R}\oplus V_{y}

with stabilising bundles
\mathbb{T}^{\mathcal{W}}_{xy}
for each
x\in\mathcal{F}
and

, along with, for each boundary face of each
\mathcal{W}_{xy}

.

We require that this data, combined with the framings for
\mathcal{F}
,
\mathcal{G}
and each lower dimensional
\mathcal{W}_{x^{\prime}y^{\prime}}

\mathcal{W}_{xy}
.

2.

Let
\mathcal{R}
be a bordism from
\mathcal{W}
to
\mathcal{W}^{\prime}
, where

on
\mathcal{R}
consists of stable isomorphisms

st^{\mathcal{R}}_{xy}:V_{x}\oplus I^{\mathcal{R}}_{xy}\to\mathbb{R}^{2}\oplus V_{y}

\mathbb{T}^{\mathcal{R}}_{xy}
along with embeddings of the stabilising bundle over each boundary face of each
\mathcal{R}_{xy}
into
\mathbb{T}^{\mathcal{R}}_{xy}
, such that these, combined with the framings for

,
\mathcal{W}^{\prime}
and each lower dimensional
\mathcal{R}_{x^{\prime}y^{\prime}}
, form systems of framings for each
\mathcal{R}_{xy}
.

3.

Let
\mathcal{W}:\mathcal{F}\to\mathcal{G}
and
\mathcal{V}:\mathcal{G}\to\mathcal{H}
be framed morphisms between framed flow categories. We form the manifolds

. A

on the collection of these manifolds
\tilde{\mathcal{Q}}

st^{\tilde{\mathcal{Q}}}_{xz}:V_{x}\oplus I^{\tilde{\mathcal{Q}}}_{xz}\to\mathbb{R}^{2}\oplus V_{z}

with stabilising bundles
\mathbb{T}^{\tilde{\mathcal{Q}}}_{xz}
along with embeddings of the stabilising bundle over each boundary face of
\tilde{\mathcal{Q}}_{xz}
into
\mathbb{T}^{\tilde{\mathcal{Q}}}_{xz}
such that, combined with the framings for
\mathcal{F}
,
\mathcal{G}
,
\mathcal{W}
,
\mathcal{V}
and each lower dimensional
\tilde{\mathcal{Q}}_{x^{\prime}y^{\prime}}
, form systems of framings for each
\tilde{\mathcal{Q}}_{xz}
.

4.

Let
\mathcal{W},\mathcal{W}^{\prime}:\mathcal{F}\to\mathcal{G}
and
\mathcal{V}:\mathcal{G}\to\mathcal{H}
be framed morphisms between framed flow categories. Assume that we are given framings on
\tilde{\mathcal{Q}}:=\tilde{\mathcal{Q}}(\mathcal{W},\mathcal{V})
and
\tilde{\mathcal{Q}}^{\prime}:=\tilde{\mathcal{Q}}(\mathcal{W}^{\prime},\mathcal{V})
. We form the manifolds
\tilde{\mathcal{S}}_{xz}:=\tilde{\mathcal{S}}_{xz}(\mathcal{R},\mathcal{V})
as in Lemma
56
. A
framing
on the collection of these manifolds
\tilde{\mathcal{S}}
consists of stable isomorphisms

st^{\tilde{\mathcal{S}}}_{xz}:V_{x}\oplus I^{\tilde{\mathcal{S}}}_{xz}\to\mathbb{R}^{3}\oplus V_{z}

with stabilising bundles
\mathbb{T}^{\tilde{\mathcal{S}}}_{xz}
along with embeddings of the stabilising bundle over each boundary face of
\tilde{\mathcal{S}}_{xz}
into
\mathbb{T}^{\tilde{\mathcal{S}}}_{xz}
such that, when combined with all other framings in sight, form systems of framings for each manifold in sight.

For expository purposes, we spell out what this means more explicitly in the simplest cases.

Example 4.12
.

Let
\mathcal{W}:\mathcal{F}\to\mathcal{G}
be a morphism between two framed flow categories. Note all boundary faces of each
\mathcal{W}_{xy}
are broken. Given stable isomorphisms

st^{\mathcal{W}}_{xy}:V_{x}\oplus I^{\mathcal{W}}_{xy}\to\mathbb{R}\oplus V_{y}

with stabilising bundles
\mathbb{T}^{\mathcal{W}}_{xy}
, the compatibility requirements for them to form a framing on
\mathcal{W}
is that the following.

We require diagrams analagous to (

) to commute over each
\mathcal{W}_{xx^{\prime}x^{\prime\prime};y}

\mathcal{W}_{xx^{\prime};y^{\prime}y}
and
\mathcal{W}_{x;y^{\prime\prime}y^{\prime}y}
. Similarly to (

) over each
\mathcal{W}_{xx^{\prime};y}
and

respectively, where we extend the compositions going right then down along the appropriate embedding of stabilising bundles:

\textstyle{V_{x}\oplus I^{\mathcal{F}}_{xx^{\prime}}\oplus I^{\mathcal{W}}_{x^{\prime}y}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{st^{\mathcal{F}}}
\scriptstyle{\psi}
\textstyle{V_{x^{\prime}}\oplus I^{\mathcal{W}}_{x^{\prime}y}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{st^{\mathcal{W}}}
\textstyle{V_{x}\oplus I^{\mathcal{W}}_{xy}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}

\textstyle{\mathbb{R}\oplus V_{y}}
\textstyle{V_{x}\oplus I^{\mathcal{W}}_{xy^{\prime}}\oplus I^{\mathcal{G}}_{y^{\prime}y}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{st^{\mathcal{W}}}
\scriptstyle{\psi}
\textstyle{\mathbb{R}\oplus V_{y^{\prime}}\oplus I^{\mathcal{G}}_{y^{\prime}y}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}

\textstyle{V_{x}\oplus I^{\mathcal{W}}_{xy}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{st^{\mathcal{W}}}
\textstyle{\mathbb{R}\oplus V_{y}}

Definition 67
.

Let
\mathcal{W}:\mathcal{F}\to\mathcal{G}
be a framed morphism of framed flow categories. We define
\overline{\mathcal{W}}
to be the morphism
\mathcal{W}
, equipped with the framing

st^{\overline{\mathcal{W}}}:V_{x}\oplus I^{\mathcal{W}}_{xy}\to\mathbb{R}\oplus V_{y}

with stabilising bundles
\mathbb{T}^{\overline{\mathcal{W}}}_{xy}:=\mathbb{T}^{\mathcal{W}}_{xy}\oplus\mathbb{R}
, given by
st^{\mathcal{W}}
postcomposed with the map
-1:\mathbb{R}\to\mathbb{R}
.

Example 4.13
.

Let
\mathcal{R}
be a bordism between two framed morphisms
\mathcal{W},\mathcal{W}^{\prime}:\mathcal{F}\to\mathcal{G}
of framed flow categories. Given stable isomorphisms

st^{\mathcal{R}}_{xy}:V_{x}\oplus I^{\mathcal{R}}_{xy}\to\mathbb{R}\oplus V_{y}

the compatibility requirement for them to form a framing on
\mathcal{R}
is that the following diagrams commute.

Over each
\mathcal{R}_{xx^{\prime};y}
and
\mathcal{R}_{x;y^{\prime}y}
, diagrams similar to those in Example
4.12
commute (note that some factors of
\mathbb{R}
must be replaced with factors of
\mathbb{R}^{2}
though).

Furthermore, over each
\mathcal{W}_{xy}
:

\textstyle{V_{x}\oplus I^{\mathcal{W}}_{xy}\oplus\mathbb{R}\sigma^{\mathcal{W}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{st^{\mathcal{W}}}
\scriptstyle{\psi}
\textstyle{V_{x}\oplus I^{\mathcal{R}}_{xy}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{st^{\mathcal{R}}}
\textstyle{\mathbb{R}^{2}\oplus V_{y}}

Note that the diagonal arrow sends the line
\mathbb{R}\sigma^{\mathcal{W}}
to the first factor in
\mathbb{R}^{2}
.

Over each
\mathcal{V}_{xy}
:

\textstyle{V_{x}\oplus I^{\mathcal{V}}_{xy}\oplus\mathbb{R}\sigma^{\mathcal{V}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{st^{\mathcal{V}}}
\scriptstyle{\psi}
\textstyle{V_{x}\oplus I^{\mathcal{R}}_{xy}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{st^{\mathcal{R}}}
\textstyle{\mathbb{R}^{2}\oplus V_{y}}

The boundary faces
\mathcal{W}_{xy}
and
\mathcal{V}_{xy}
are the unbroken boundary faces of
\mathcal{R}_{xy}
.

Example 4.14
.

Let
\mathcal{W}:\mathcal{F}\to\mathcal{G}
be a framed morphism between framed flow categories, and
\mathcal{R}
the bordism from
\mathcal{W}
to itself constructed in Lemma
52
. We equip
\mathcal{R}
with a framing as follows.

We write
\mathcal{R}_{xy}=\mathcal{W}_{xy}\times[0,1]_{s}
, with
s
the coordinate on the interval. Then define

st^{\mathcal{R}}_{xy}:V_{x}\oplus I^{\mathcal{R}}_{xy}=V_{x}\oplus I^{\mathcal{W}}_{xy}\oplus\mathbb{R}\partial_{s}\xrightarrow{st^{\mathcal{W}}}\mathbb{R}^{2}\oplus V_{y}

where
\partial_{s}
is sent to
(0,1)\in\mathbb{R}^{2}
.

It is straightforward to check this is a stable framing; we spell out the details over the unbroken outgoing boundary face
\mathcal{W}_{xy}\times\{1\}
. Note that the inwards normal along this face is
-\partial_{s}
.

We must check that the following diagram commutes over
\mathcal{W}_{xy}\times\{1\}
:

\textstyle{V_{x}\oplus I^{\mathcal{W}}_{xy}\oplus\mathbb{R}\sigma\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\psi}
\scriptstyle{st^{\mathcal{W}}}
\textstyle{V_{x}\oplus I^{\mathcal{R}}_{xy}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{st^{\mathcal{R}}}
\textstyle{\mathbb{R}^{2}\oplus V_{y}}

Both ways around the diagram send
\sigma
to
(0,1)\in\mathbb{R}^{2}
, and agree on the other factors by construction of
st^{\mathcal{R}}
.

Example 4.15
.

Homotopic framings on the same morphism are framed bordant.

Example 4.16
.

Let
\mathcal{W}:\mathcal{F}\to\mathcal{G}
be a framed morphism, and let
E
be some vector space. We define a framed morphism
\mathcal{W}^{E}:\mathcal{F}\to\mathcal{G}
as follows: the underlying unframed morphism of
\mathcal{W}^{E}
is defined to be the same as that of
\mathcal{W}
. We define stabilising bundles
\mathbb{T}^{\mathcal{W}^{E}}_{xy}:=\mathbb{T}^{\mathcal{W}}_{xy}\oplus E
, and we define the stable framings of
\mathcal{W}^{E}
to be those of
\mathcal{W}
extended under the natural inclusions
\mathbb{T}^{\mathcal{W}}_{xy}\to\mathbb{T}^{\mathcal{W}^{E}}_{xy}
. Then
\mathcal{W}
and
\mathcal{W}^{E}
are framed bordant.

More generally, if
E_{xy}\to\mathcal{W}_{xy}
are vector bundles and we are given embeddings
E_{x^{\prime}y}\to E_{xy}
and
E_{xy^{\prime}}\to E_{xy}
over each
\mathcal{W}_{xx^{\prime};y}
and
\mathcal{W}_{x;y^{\prime}y}
respectively which are suitably associative, we obtain another framed morphism
\mathcal{W}^{E}
with stabilising bundles
\mathbb{T}^{\mathcal{W}^{E}}_{xy}:=\mathcal{W}_{xy}\oplus E_{xy}
, given by extending the framings for
\mathcal{W}
along the natural inclusions
\mathbb{T}^{\mathcal{W}}_{xy}\to\mathbb{T}^{\mathcal{W}^{E}}_{xy}
. Once again,
\mathcal{W}^{E}
is framed bordant to
\mathcal{W}
.

We call the data of the
E_{xy}
above a
system of vector bundles
on
\mathcal{W}
, and a framed morphism of the form
\mathcal{W}^{E}
a
stabilisation
of
\mathcal{W}
along
E
. One can similarly define systems of vector bundles, and stabilisations along them, for the other objects defined in Definition
66
.

Definition 68
.

Let
\mathcal{F}
and
\mathcal{G}
be framed flow categories. We define
[\mathcal{F},\mathcal{G}]^{fr}
to be the set of framed morphisms
\mathcal{F}\to\mathcal{G}
, modulo the equivalence relation generated by

1.

\mathcal{W}\sim\mathcal{V}
if
\mathcal{W}
,
\mathcal{V}
are framed bordant.

2.

\mathcal{W}\sim\mathcal{V}
if
\mathcal{W}
and
\mathcal{V}
are diffeomorphic, via a diffeomorphism compatible with the framings.

Similarly to
[\mathcal{F},\mathcal{G}]
, it is clear that
[\mathcal{F},\mathcal{G}]^{fr}
forms a commutative monoid under disjoint union, with unit given by the empty morphism.

Lemma 69
.

Inverses exist in
[\mathcal{F},\mathcal{G}]^{fr}
, i.e. it’s a group.

Proof 4.17
.

Let
\mathcal{W}^{\mathbb{R}}
be as constructed in Example
4.16
; as noted before, this is framed bordant to
\mathcal{W}
. Then the framed bordism constructed in Example
4.14
can alternatively be viewed as a framed nullbordism of
\mathcal{W}^{\mathbb{R}}\sqcup\overline{\mathcal{W}}
, implying that the inverse of
\mathcal{W}
is
\overline{\mathcal{W}}
.

Lemma 70
.

Let
\mathcal{W},\mathcal{W}^{\prime}:\mathcal{F}\to\mathcal{G}
and
\mathcal{V}:\mathcal{G}\to\mathcal{H}
be framed morphisms of framed flow categories. Let
\tilde{\mathcal{Q}}:=\tilde{\mathcal{Q}}(\mathcal{W},\mathcal{V})
, let
\tilde{\mathcal{Q}}^{\prime}:=\tilde{\mathcal{Q}}(\mathcal{W}^{\prime},\mathcal{V})
and let
\tilde{\mathcal{S}}:=\tilde{\mathcal{S}}(\mathcal{R},\mathcal{V})
.

Then
\tilde{\mathcal{Q}},\tilde{\mathcal{Q}}^{\prime}
and
\tilde{\mathcal{S}}
admit framings, canonical up to stabilisation and isomorphism of framings.

Proof 4.18
.

We first frame
\tilde{\mathcal{Q}}

Inductively over the index difference

\textstyle{\mathbb{T}^{\mathcal{W}\mathcal{G}\mathcal{V}}_{xyy^{\prime}z}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}

\textstyle{\mathbb{T}^{\tilde{Q}}_{xz}}

Note that the choice of these bundles and embeddings is unique up to stabilisation and isomorphism.

We define stable isomorphisms

We must check these agree on overlaps for this to define a stable framing over the entirety of

to
\tau_{y}
and
\tau_{z}
to
\tau_{z}
. Then noting that we can identify the domain with

\textstyle{I^{\tilde{\mathcal{Q}}}_{xz}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}

\scriptstyle{\psi^{-1}}

Lemma 71
.

Let
\mathcal{W}:\mathcal{F}\to\mathcal{G}
and
\mathcal{V}:\mathcal{G}\to\mathcal{H}
be framed morphisms of framed flow categories. Then their composition can be framed, and the equivalence class
[\mathcal{V}\circ\mathcal{W}]
defines in
[\mathcal{F},\mathcal{H}]^{fr}
is well-defined.

Proof 4.19
.

Compatibly choose framed functions
(f_{xz},s_{xz})
on each
\tilde{\mathcal{Q}}_{xz}
, let
\tilde{\mathcal{Q}}^{\leq 1}=f_{xz}^{-1}(-\infty,1]
and let
\mathcal{U}_{xz}=f_{xz}^{-1}\{1\}
. Then
\mathcal{U}
is a representative of
\mathcal{V}\circ\mathcal{W}
as an unframed morphism; we use the framing we’ve constructed on
\tilde{\mathcal{Q}}
to frame
\mathcal{U}
.

We define stable isomorphisms
st^{\mathcal{U}}_{xz}:I^{\mathcal{U}}_{xz}\oplus V_{z}\to\mathbb{R}\oplus V_{x}
with stabilising bundle
\mathbb{T}^{\tilde{\mathcal{Q}}}_{xz}\oplus\mathbb{R}\xi
(where
\xi
is some formal generator) to be the composition:

st^{\mathcal{U}}_{xz}:V_{x}\oplus I^{\mathcal{U}}_{xz}\oplus\mathbb{R}\xi\xrightarrow{di_{xz}+s_{xz}}V_{x}\oplus I^{\tilde{\mathcal{Q}}}_{xz}\xrightarrow{st^{\tilde{\mathcal{Q}}}}\mathbb{R}^{2}\oplus V_{z}=\mathbb{R}\oplus\mathbb{R}\xi\oplus V_{z}

Here
i_{xz}:\mathcal{U}_{xz}\to\tilde{\mathcal{Q}}_{xz}
is the natural inclusion map,
s_{xz}
takes
\mathbb{R}\xi
as input, the first map sends
\tau_{z}
to
\tau_{z}
, and the final identification is to make it clear that it is the second copy of
\mathbb{R}
in
\mathbb{R}^{2}
that is the stabilising bundle.

Commutativity of

⋆

‣
65
over the appropriate boundary faces follows from similar considerations to before, though now we must use the compatibility of the framed functions with those on the boundary faces. This implies
\mathcal{U}
is a framed morphism.

Since the framing on
\tilde{\mathcal{Q}}
was canonical up to stabilisation and isomorphism, it follows from construction that (for fixed choice of framed functions
(f_{xz},s_{xz})
) the induced framing on
\mathcal{U}
is also canonical up to stabilisation and isomorphism.

Similarly we can frame
\tilde{\mathcal{Q}}^{\leq 1}
in an appropriate sense; note commutativity of
65
uses the fact that we identify
\mathbb{R}^{2}=\mathbb{R}\oplus\mathbb{R}\xi
rather than the other way around. Proceeding as in the proof of Lemma
55
(but now with framings in hand) shows that the class of
\mathcal{U}
in
[\mathcal{F},\mathcal{H}]^{fr}
is independent of choices.

Lemma 72
.

Composition of framed morphisms is compatible with the equivalence relation in Definition
68
, and induces a well-defined bilinear map

\circ:[\mathcal{G},\mathcal{H}]^{fr}\otimes[\mathcal{F},\mathcal{G}]^{fr}\to[\mathcal{F},\mathcal{H}]^{fr}

Proof 4.20
.

Similarly to the proof of Lemma
71
, we choose compatible framed functions on each
\tilde{\mathcal{Q}}_{xz}
and
\tilde{\mathcal{S}}_{xz}
, and proceed as in the proof of Lemma
55
, framing the bordism
\mathcal{T}
similarly to above, to conclude that composition induces the desired well-defined map; the same argument as in the proof of Lemma
55
also shows it is a bilinear map.

Similarly to Lemma
57
, we have:

Lemma 73
.

The composition map is associative, i.e. the following diagram commutes:

\textstyle{\left[\mathcal{H},\mathcal{I}\right]^{fr}\otimes\left[\mathcal{G},\mathcal{H}\right]^{fr}\otimes\left[\mathcal{F},\mathcal{G}\right]^{fr}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\circ}
\scriptstyle{\circ}
\textstyle{\left[\mathcal{G},\mathcal{I}\right]^{fr}\otimes\left[\mathcal{F},\mathcal{G}\right]^{fr}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\circ}
\textstyle{\left[\mathcal{H},\mathcal{I}\right]^{fr}\otimes\left[\mathcal{F},\mathcal{H}\right]^{fr}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\circ}
\textstyle{\left[\mathcal{F},\mathcal{I}\right]^{fr}}

Proof 4.21
.

We prove this by equipping all the manifolds encountered in the proof of Lemma
57
with (compatible) stable framings; we furthermore assume all of the same notation as in Lemma
57
.

Using the framings on
\mathcal{F}
,
\mathcal{G}
,
\mathcal{H}
,
\mathcal{I}
,
\mathcal{W}
,
\mathcal{V}
and
\mathcal{X}
, we equip
\tilde{\mathcal{P}}
,
\tilde{\mathcal{P}}^{\prime}
,
\tilde{\mathcal{Q}}
,
\tilde{\mathcal{Q}}^{\prime}
and
\tilde{\mathcal{Z}}
with stable framings, just as in Lemma
71
.

Using the given choices of framed functions, we equip
\mathcal{M}
,
\mathcal{M}^{\prime}
,
\mathcal{U}
,
\mathcal{U}^{\prime}
,
\mathcal{Y}
,
\mathcal{L}
all with stable framings.

Unfortunately the embeddings from the collar neighbourhood theorem
\mathcal{P}_{xw},\mathcal{P}^{\prime}_{xw}\hookrightarrow\mathcal{Y}_{xw}
may not be compatible with the stable framings, so whilst
\mathcal{L}
is a bordism between
\mathcal{M}
and
\mathcal{M}^{\prime}
, the framing we have equipped
\mathcal{L}
with might not be compatible with the framings on
\mathcal{M}
and
\mathcal{M}^{\prime}
, so it might not be a framed bordism between them.

However note that the framings on each
\tilde{\mathcal{P}}_{xw}
agree with those on
\mathcal{Y}_{xw}
when restricted to the carapace of
\tilde{\mathcal{P}}_{xw}
. Since each
\tilde{\mathcal{P}}_{xw}
deformation retracts to its carapace (which also includes all its boundary), the framings on
\tilde{\mathcal{P}}_{xw}
pulled back from
\mathcal{Y}_{xw}
are homotopic to those constructed on the
\tilde{\mathcal{P}}_{xw}
directly; furthermore by inducting on the dimension and working relative to the boundary we may assume that these homotopies are compatible with each other for all
x\in\mathcal{F}
,
w\in\mathcal{I}
.

Since homotopic framings on
\tilde{\mathcal{P}}_{xw}
induce homotopic framings on
\mathcal{M}_{xw}
(since the construction of this framing works in 1-parameter families of framings on
\tilde{\mathcal{P}}
), and noting that this discussion applies equally to
\tilde{\mathcal{P}}^{\prime}
and
\mathcal{M}^{\prime}
, it follows that
\mathcal{L}
is a framed bordism between
\mathcal{M}
and
\mathcal{M}^{\prime}
, where
\mathcal{M}
and
\mathcal{M}^{\prime}
are equipped with framings homotopic to the ones we originally equipped them with.

Combined with Example
4.15
, it follows that
\mathcal{M}
and
\mathcal{M}^{\prime}
are framed bordant.

Similarly to Lemma
58
(and by the same proof, but incorporating the ‘deformation to the carapace’ argument from above), we have:

Lemma 74
.

In the situation of Lemma
58
, if
\mathcal{F}
,
\mathcal{G}
,
\mathcal{H}
,
\mathcal{W}
,
\mathcal{V}
,
\mathcal{T}
and
\mathcal{R}
are all compatibly framed, then
\mathcal{T}
is a representative of
\mathcal{V}\circ\mathcal{W}
in
[\mathcal{F},\mathcal{H}]^{fr}
.

Definition 75
.

We define the (homotopy)
category of framed flow categories
,
\Flow^{fr}
, to have objects framed flow categories, and morphisms framed morphisms of flow categories. This is a (non-unital) category enriched in abelian groups.

Example 4.22
.

Given a framed flow category
\mathcal{F}
, we can form
\mathcal{F}[n]
as a framed flow category for any
n\in\mathbb{Z}
, by setting
V^{\mathcal{F}[n]}_{x}:=V^{\mathcal{F}}_{x}\oplus\mathbb{R}^{n}
and
st^{\mathcal{F}[n]}:=st^{\mathcal{F}}\oplus\id_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}
.

Example 4.23
.

A morphism
*[i]\to*
consists of a closed (stably) framed
i
-manifold; a framed bordism between morphisms consists of a (stably) framed bordism. From this, we see that

[*[i],*]^{fr}=\Omega^{fr}_{i}(\ast)=\Omega^{fr}_{i}

In particular, it follows that
\Flow^{fr}
is enriched over graded modules over
\Omega^{fr}_{*}
.

Conjecture 76

(Abouzaid-Blumberg)
.

\Flow^{fr}
is equivalent to the homotopy category of finite spectra.

Though not phrased like this, a functor from the category of framed flow categories to the homotopy category of finite spectra is essentially constructed in
[
Lar
]
, following
[
CJS95
]
.

5
Algebra over flow categories

5.1
Bilinear maps of flow categories

Definition 77
.

Let
\mathcal{F}
,
\mathcal{G}
and
\mathcal{H}
be flow categories. A
bilinear map
of flow categories
\mathcal{W}:\mathcal{F}\times\mathcal{G}\rightarrow\mathcal{H}
consists of compact smooth manifolds with faces
\mathcal{W}_{xy;z}
of dimension
|x|+|y|-|z|
for each
x
in
\mathcal{F}
,
y
in
\mathcal{G}
and
z
in
\mathcal{H}
, along with maps

c=c^{\mathcal{W}}:\mathcal{F}_{xu}\times\mathcal{W}_{uy;z}\rightarrow\mathcal{W}_{xy;z}

and

c=c^{\mathcal{W}}:\mathcal{G}_{yv}\times\mathcal{W}_{xv;z}\rightarrow\mathcal{H}_{xy;z}

and

c=c^{\mathcal{W}}:\mathcal{W}_{xy;w}\times\mathcal{H}_{wz}\rightarrow\mathcal{W}_{xy;z}

which are compatible with each other and
c^{\mathcal{F}}
,
c^{\mathcal{G}}
and
c^{\mathcal{H}}
, and which define a system of faces

\mathcal{F}_{x_{0}\ldots x_{i}}\times\mathcal{G}_{y_{0}\ldots y_{j}}\times\mathcal{W}_{x_{i}y_{j};z_{0}}\times\mathcal{H}_{z_{0}\ldots z_{k}}

for
x_{0}=x,\ldots,x_{i}
in
\mathcal{F}
,
y_{0}=y,\ldots,y_{j}
in
\mathcal{G}
and
z_{0},\ldots,z_{k}=z
in
\mathcal{H}
. In particular,
\mathcal{W}_{xy;z}
has a system of boundary faces
\mathcal{F}_{xx^{\prime}}\times\mathcal{W}_{x^{\prime}y;z}
,
\mathcal{G}_{yy^{\prime}}\times\mathcal{W}_{xy^{\prime};z}
and
\mathcal{W}_{xy;z^{\prime}}\times\mathcal{H}_{z^{\prime}z}
for
x^{\prime}\in\mathcal{F}
,
y^{\prime}\in\mathcal{G}
and
z^{\prime}\in\mathcal{H}
.

{ex}

A bilinear map
*[i]\times*[j]\rightarrow\mathcal{H}
consists of the same data as a morphism
*[i+j]\rightarrow\mathcal{H}
.

{ex}

A bilinear map
*[i]\times\mathcal{G}\to\mathcal{H}
consists of the same data as a morphism
\mathcal{G}[i]\to\mathcal{H}
.

Let
\mathcal{F}
,
\mathcal{G}
and
\mathcal{H}
be flow categories,
\mathcal{W}:\mathcal{F}\times\mathcal{G}\rightarrow\mathcal{H}
a bilinear map and
\mathcal{A}:*[i]\rightarrow\mathcal{F}
and
\mathcal{B}:*[j]\rightarrow\mathcal{G}
morphisms.
We define a morphism of flow categories

\mathcal{W}\circ\mathcal{A}:\mathcal{G}[i]\rightarrow\mathcal{H}

as follows.

For
y
in
\mathcal{G}
and
z
in
\mathcal{H}
, define

\tilde{\mathcal{P}}_{yz}:=\tilde{\mathcal{P}}_{yz}(\mathcal{A},\mathcal{W})=\left(\bigsqcup_{x\in\mathcal{F}}\mathcal{A}_{*x}\times\mathcal{W}_{xy;z}\times[0,\varepsilon)\right)/\sim

where
\sim
is defined so that
\tilde{\mathcal{P}}_{yz}
is the coequaliser of

\bigsqcup_{x,x^{\prime}\in\mathcal{F}}\mathcal{A}_{*x}\times\mathcal{F}_{xx^{\prime}}\times\mathcal{W}_{x^{\prime}y;z}\times[0,\varepsilon)^{2}_{u,v}\rightrightarrows\bigsqcup_{x\in\mathcal{F}}\mathcal{A}_{*x}\times\mathcal{W}_{xy;z}\times[0,\varepsilon)_{s}

where the two maps send
(a,b,c,u,v)
to
(a,\mathcal{C}(b,c,u),v)
and
(\mathcal{C}(a,b,v),c,u)
respectively, as in the construction of the composition of morphisms.

By Lemmas
37
and
38
(applying these lemmas with
I=ob\mathcal{F}
and
J=ob\mathcal{G}\sqcup ob\mathcal{H}
), this is a manifold with faces, with a system of faces given by

\mathcal{A}_{*;x_{0}\ldots x_{i}}\times\mathcal{G}_{y_{0}\ldots y_{j}}\times\mathcal{W}_{x_{i}y_{j};z_{0}}\times\mathcal{H}_{z_{0}\ldots z_{k}}\times\{0\}

and

\mathcal{G}_{y_{0}\ldots y_{j}}\times\tilde{\mathcal{P}}_{y_{j}z_{0}}\times\mathcal{H}_{z_{0}\ldots z_{k}}

for
x,x_{0},\ldots,x_{i}
in
\mathcal{F}
,
y_{0}=y,\ldots,y_{j}
in
\mathcal{G}
and
z_{0},\ldots,z_{k}=z
in
\mathcal{H}
. Pick framed functions
(f_{yz},s_{yz})
on each
\tilde{\mathcal{P}}_{yz}
which are compatible with each other on overlaps, and let
\mathcal{V}_{yz}:=f^{-1}_{yz}\{1\}
for all
y\in\mathcal{G}
,
z\in\mathcal{H}
. These form a morphism of flow categories
\mathcal{G}[i]\rightarrow\mathcal{H}
which we define
\mathcal{W}\circ\mathcal{A}
to be. By the same argument as in the proof of Lemma
55
, this defines a well-defined morphism in
\Flow
.

Given instead
\mathcal{B}:*[j]\rightarrow\mathcal{G}
, we can similarly define
\tilde{\mathcal{P}}_{xz}=\tilde{\mathcal{P}}_{xz}(\mathcal{B},\mathcal{W})
for
x\in\mathcal{F}
and
z\in\mathcal{H}
, and define a morphism of flow categories

\mathcal{W}\circ\mathcal{B}:\mathcal{F}[j]\rightarrow\mathcal{H}

Lemma 78
.

For all morphisms of flow categories
\mathcal{A}:*[i]\to\mathcal{F}
and
\mathcal{B}:*[j]\to\mathcal{G}
, we have that

[(\mathcal{W}\circ\mathcal{A})\circ\mathcal{B}]=[(\mathcal{W}\circ\mathcal{B})\circ\mathcal{A}]\in[*[i+j],\mathcal{H}]

Proof 5.1
.

For
z\in\mathcal{H}
, define
\tilde{\mathcal{Q}}_{*z}(\mathcal{B},\tilde{\mathcal{P}}(\mathcal{A},\mathcal{W}))
to be the coequaliser of the following diagram:

\bigsqcup\limits_{y,y^{\prime}\in\mathcal{G}}\mathcal{B}_{*y}\times\mathcal{G}_{yy^{\prime}}\times\tilde{\mathcal{P}}_{y^{\prime}z}\times[0,\varepsilon)^{2}_{u,v}\rightrightarrows\bigsqcup\limits_{y\in\mathcal{G}}\mathcal{B}_{*y}\times\tilde{\mathcal{P}}_{yz}\times[0,\varepsilon)_{s}

where the two maps send
(a,b,c,u,v)
to
(a,\mathcal{C}(b,c,u),v)
and
(\mathcal{C}(a,b,v),c,u)
respectively, as before.

By Lemmas
37
and
38
,
\tilde{\mathcal{Q}}_{*z}(\mathcal{B},\tilde{\mathcal{P}}(\mathcal{A},\mathcal{W}))
has a system of boundary faces given by
\tilde{\mathcal{Q}}_{*z^{\prime}}\times\mathcal{H}_{z^{\prime}z}
,
\mathcal{A}_{*x}\times\{0\}\times\tilde{\mathcal{P}}_{xz}
and
{\mathcal{B}}_{*y}\times\tilde{\mathcal{P}}_{yz}\times\{0\}
for
x\in\mathcal{F}
,
y\in\mathcal{G}
and
z^{\prime}\in\mathcal{H}
; we note that the final two types of boundary faces overlap in codimension two faces of the form
\mathcal{A}_{*x}\times\mathcal{B}_{*y}\times\mathcal{W}_{xy;z}\times\{(0,0)\}
, which is a compact face.

Choose framed functions
(f_{xy},s_{xy})
and
(f_{yz},s_{yz})
on each
\tilde{\mathcal{P}}_{xz}
and
\tilde{\mathcal{P}}_{yz}
respectively, and let
\mathcal{U}_{xz}=f_{xz}^{-1}\{1\}
and
\mathcal{U}_{yz}=f_{yz}^{-1}\{1\}
respectively; these are representatives of
\mathcal{W}\circ\mathcal{B}
and
\mathcal{W}\circ\mathcal{A}
respectively.

Now choose framed functions
(g_{*z},t_{*z})
on each
\tilde{\mathcal{Q}}_{*z}
, extending the
(f_{xy},s_{xy})
and
(f_{yz},s_{yz})
on appropriate boundary faces. Then considering
g_{*z}^{-1}\{1\}
and using the argument in Lemma
58
, the result follows.

Definition 79
.

We define a map

\mathcal{W}_{*}:[*[i],\mathcal{F}]\otimes[*[j],\mathcal{G}]\rightarrow[*[i+j],\mathcal{H}]

sending
[\mathcal{A}]\otimes[\mathcal{B}]
to
[(\mathcal{W}\circ\mathcal{A})\circ\mathcal{B}]=[(\mathcal{W}\circ\mathcal{B})\circ\mathcal{A}]\in[*[i+j],\mathcal{H}]
. This is a well-defined map, by Lemmas
78
and
56
.

Lemma 80
.

\mathcal{W}_{*}
is a bilinear map.

Proof 5.2
.

Since composition is bilinear,
[(\mathcal{W}\circ\mathcal{A})\circ\mathcal{B}]
is linear in
[\mathcal{B}]
. Similarly
[(\mathcal{W}\circ\mathcal{B})\circ\mathcal{A}]
is linear in
[\mathcal{A}]
.

Definition 81
.

Let
\mathcal{W},\mathcal{V}:\mathcal{F}\times\mathcal{G}\to\mathcal{H}
be bilinear maps. A
bordism
between
\mathcal{W}
and
\mathcal{V}
consists of compact manifolds with faces
\mathcal{R}_{xy;z}
of dimension
|x|+|y|-|z|+1
for each
x\in\mathcal{F}
,
y\in\mathcal{G}
and
z\in\mathcal{H}
, with systems of boundary faces given by
\mathcal{F}_{xx^{\prime}}\times\mathcal{R}_{x^{\prime}y;z}
,
\mathcal{G}_{yy^{\prime}}\times\mathcal{R}_{xy^{\prime};z}
,
\mathcal{R}_{xz;z^{\prime}}\times\mathcal{H}_{z^{\prime}z}
,
\mathcal{W}_{xy;z}
and
\mathcal{V}_{xy;z}
. Note all of these are broken except the last two which are unbroken; we label them as incoming and outgoing respectively.

In both cases, as usual we that the inclusions
c
of each boundary face satisfy appropriate associativity relations.

If
\mathcal{W}:\mathcal{F}\times\mathcal{G}\to\mathcal{H}
is a bilinear map and
\mathcal{A}:\mathcal{F}^{\prime}\to\mathcal{F}
is a morphism, we can form another bilinear map
\mathcal{W}\circ\mathcal{A}:\mathcal{F}^{\prime}\times\mathcal{G}\to\mathcal{H}
in the same way as in the previously treated case
\mathcal{F}=*[i]
: for
w\in\mathcal{F}^{\prime}
,
y\in\mathcal{G}
and
z\in\mathcal{H}
, we let
\tilde{\mathcal{P}}_{wy;z}=\tilde{\mathcal{P}}_{wy;z}(\mathcal{A},\mathcal{W})
be the coequaliser of

\bigsqcup_{x,x^{\prime}\in\mathcal{F}}\mathcal{A}_{wx}\times\mathcal{F}_{xx^{\prime}}\times\mathcal{W}_{x^{\prime}y;z}\times[0,\varepsilon)^{2}_{u,v}\rightrightarrows\bigsqcup_{x\in\mathcal{F}}\mathcal{A}_{wx}\times\mathcal{W}_{xy;z}\times[0,\varepsilon)_{s}

Then choosing appropriate compatible framed functions and taking the preimage of 1 gives a bilinear map
\mathcal{F}^{\prime}\times\mathcal{G}\to\mathcal{H}
; the usual arguments show this is well-defined up to bordism. Note this generalises the previous construction by Example
5.1
.

If instead we are given a morphism
\mathcal{B}:\mathcal{G}^{\prime}\to\mathcal{G}
, we can similarly form a bilinear map
\mathcal{W}\circ\mathcal{B}:\mathcal{F}\times\mathcal{G}^{\prime}\to\mathcal{H}
.

Lemma 82
.

Let
\mathcal{W},\mathcal{V}:\mathcal{F}\times\mathcal{G}\to\mathcal{H}
be bilinear maps,
\mathcal{A}:\mathcal{F}^{\prime}\to\mathcal{F}
a morphism and
\mathcal{R}
a bordism from
\mathcal{W}
to
\mathcal{V}
. Then
\mathcal{W}\circ\mathcal{A}
and
\mathcal{V}\circ\mathcal{A}
are bordant bilinear maps
\mathcal{F}^{\prime}\times\mathcal{G}\to\mathcal{H}
.

In particular,
\mathcal{W}_{*}=\mathcal{V}_{*}
.

Proof 5.3
.

For
w\in\mathcal{F}^{\prime}
,
y\in\mathcal{G}
and
z\in\mathcal{H}
, let
\tilde{\mathcal{P}}_{wy;z}=\tilde{\mathcal{P}}_{wy;z}(\mathcal{A},\mathcal{R})
be the coequaliser of

\bigsqcup_{x,x^{\prime}\in\mathcal{F}}\mathcal{A}_{wx}\times\mathcal{F}_{xx^{\prime}}\times\mathcal{R}_{x^{\prime}y;z}\times[0,\varepsilon)^{2}_{u,v}\rightrightarrows\bigsqcup_{x\in\mathcal{F}}\mathcal{A}_{wx}\times\mathcal{R}_{xy;z}\times[0,\varepsilon)_{s}

Then choosing suitable compatible framed functions
(f_{wy;z},s_{wy;z})
on each
\tilde{\mathcal{P}}_{wy;z}
extending compatible framed functions on the boundary faces
\tilde{\mathcal{P}}_{wy;z}(\mathcal{A},\mathcal{W})
and
\tilde{\mathcal{P}}_{wy;z}(\mathcal{A},\mathcal{V})
and taking the preimage of 1 provides the desired bordism.

Now let
\mathcal{F}
,
\mathcal{G}
,
\mathcal{H}
,
\mathcal{I}
,
\mathcal{J}
and
\mathcal{K}
be flow categories, and let
\mathcal{W}:\mathcal{F}\times\mathcal{G}\rightarrow\mathcal{I}
,
\mathcal{V}:\mathcal{G}\times\mathcal{H}\rightarrow\mathcal{J}
,
\mathcal{X}:\mathcal{I}\times\mathcal{H}\rightarrow\mathcal{K}
and
\mathcal{Y}:\mathcal{F}\times\mathcal{J}\rightarrow\mathcal{K}
be bilinear maps. These induce trilinear maps of abelian groups

\mathcal{X}_{*}\circ\mathcal{W}_{*},\mathcal{Y}_{*}\circ\mathcal{V}_{*}:[*[i],\mathcal{F}]\otimes[*[j],\mathcal{G}]\otimes[*[k],\mathcal{H}]\rightarrow[*[i+j+k],\mathcal{K}]

We will give conditions for these two maps to agree.

Definition 83
.

An
associator

\mathcal{Z}
for the decuplet
(\mathcal{F},\mathcal{G},\mathcal{H},\mathcal{I},\mathcal{J},\mathcal{K},\mathcal{W},\mathcal{V},\mathcal{X},\mathcal{Y})
consists of compact manifolds with faces
\mathcal{Z}_{xyz;w}
for
x
in
\mathcal{F}
,
y
in
\mathcal{G}
,
z
in
\mathcal{H}
and
w
in
\mathcal{I}
, of dimension
|x|+|y|+|z|-|w|+1
, with a system of faces given by

\mathcal{F}_{x_{0}\ldots x_{i}}\times\mathcal{G}_{y_{0}\ldots y_{j}}\times\mathcal{H}_{z_{0}\ldots z_{k}}\times\mathcal{Z}_{x_{i}y_{j}z_{k};w_{0}}\times\mathcal{K}_{w_{0}\ldots w_{l}}

and

\mathcal{F}_{x_{0}\ldots x_{i}}\times\mathcal{G}_{y_{0}\ldots y_{j}}\times\mathcal{H}_{z_{0}\ldots z_{k}}\times\mathcal{W}_{x_{i}y_{j};u_{0}}\times\mathcal{I}_{u_{0}\ldots u_{r}}\times\mathcal{X}_{u_{r}z_{k};w_{0}}\times\mathcal{K}_{w_{0}\ldots w_{l}}

and

\mathcal{F}_{x_{0}\ldots x_{i}}\times\mathcal{G}_{y_{0}\ldots y_{j}}\times\mathcal{H}_{z_{0}\ldots z_{k}}\times\mathcal{V}_{y_{j}z_{k};v_{0}}\times\mathcal{J}_{v_{0}\ldots v_{s}}\times\mathcal{Y}_{x_{i}v_{j};w_{0}}\times\mathcal{K}_{w_{0}\ldots w_{l}}

for
x_{0}=x,\ldots,x_{i}
in
\mathcal{F}
,
