, along with a map
f:N\times Q\to X
which is constant in the
Q
-direction, represents a class in
\ker\theta
, since it bounds the
\kBS
-manifold with faces
N\times Q\times[0,1]
where
\iota
is the inclusion
N\times Q\times\{1\}\hookrightarrow N\times Q\times[0,1]
, along with the map to
X
given by
f
.

6.3
Framings and singularities

We would like to equip our manifolds with singularities with framings which are suitably compatible with the singularities. Roughly speaking, a stable framing on a manifold with
\kBS
-singularities consists of a stable framing on the underlying manifold with faces, which respects the product structure over each singular face. Note however that “respecting the product structure” will in fact consist of more data.

Definition 124
.

Let
M=(M,N_{P},\iota)
be a
\kBS
-manifold with faces. We treat each singular boundary face
N_{P}\times P
as an unbroken face.

Given a stable isomorphism
E\oplus TM\to F
with stabilising bundle
\mathbb{T}
, we say it’s
compatible with the singularities
if we are also given embeddings
\mathbb{T}^{P}\to\mathbb{T}
over each
N_{P}\times P
, along with stable isomorphisms
st^{N}_{P}:E\oplus TN_{P}\oplus\mathbb{R}\sigma_{P}\to F-\mathbb{R}^{k}
with stabilising bundle the orthogonal complement of
\mathbb{T}^{P}
, such that the following diagram commutes over each
N_{P}\times P
:

\textstyle{E\oplus TN_{P}\oplus\mathbb{R}\sigma_{P}\oplus TP\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\psi}
\scriptstyle{st^{N}_{P}\oplus st^{P}}
\textstyle{F-\mathbb{R}^{k}\oplus\mathbb{R}^{k}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{E\oplus TM\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{F}

where the right vertical arrow uses the natural stable isomorphism
-\mathbb{R}^{k}\oplus\mathbb{R}^{k}\to 0
with stabilising bundle 0. Note that this definition requires the extra data of the
st^{N}_{P}
.

From this, we can define the framed bordism groups with
\kBS
-singularities, which we denote by
\Omega^{fr;\kBS}_{*}(X)
for any space
X
. As before, there is a natural map
\Omega^{fr}_{*}(X)\to\Omega^{fr;\kBS}_{*}(X)
, though
\Omega^{fr;\kBS}_{*}:=\Omega^{fr;\kBS}_{*}(*)
is not a ring.

Similarly to Proposition
122
and by the same proof with framings incorporated, we have

Proposition 125
.

Let
X
be any space. The group
\Omega_{k}^{fr;\kBS}(X)
is zero.

Proposition 126
.

The natural map
\Omega_{k+1}^{fr}(X)\to\Omega_{k+1}^{fr;\kBS}(X)
has kernel
\Omega_{k}^{fr}(\ast)\cdot\Omega_{1}^{fr}(X)
.

Proof 6.7
.

The proof is a minor variation on that of Proposition
123
. If
M
is a framed closed
\kBS
-manifold and a map
f:M\to X
, such that
(M,f)
vanishes in
\Omega_{k+1}^{fr;\kBS}(X)
, then there is some framed
\kBS
-manifold
(W,N_{P},\iota)
mapping to
X
, with
M
as its only essential face, and such that the framing on
W
and the map
W\to X
are both compatible with the singularities. Thus, since the framing is product-like on each
N_{P}\times P
and the map to
X
is constant on
P
, it follows that
(M,f)
lies in
\Omega_{k}^{fr}(\ast)\cdot\Omega_{1}^{fr}(X)
. The other direction is exactly as in
op.cit.

Definition 127
.

Let
\mathcal{S}:\mathcal{F}\to\mathcal{G}
be a
\kBS
-
\tau_{\leq n}
-morphism (respectively bordism). A
framing
on
\mathcal{S}
consists of the same data as a framing on a non-singular morphism (resp. bordism), along with the extra data that each stable framing
V_{x}\oplus I^{\mathcal{S}}_{xy}\to\mathbb{R}^{i}\oplus V_{y}
, where
i=1
(resp.
i=2
), is compatible with the singularities, and that the embeddings
\mathbb{T}^{P}\to\mathbb{T}^{\mathcal{S}}_{xy}
over each singular face are compatible with the embeddings
\mathbb{T}^{\mathcal{S}}_{xx^{\prime}y},\mathbb{T}^{\mathcal{S}}_{xy^{\prime}y}\to\mathbb{T}^{\mathcal{S}}_{xy}
.

For concreteness, we spell out what this definition says explicitly.

Write
\mathcal{S}_{xy}=(\mathcal{S}_{xy},N^{\mathcal{S}}_{P;xy},\iota^{\mathcal{S}}_{xy})
for the structure of a
\kBS
-manifold on each
\mathcal{S}_{xy}
and let
I^{N^{\mathcal{S}}}_{P;xy}:=TN^{\mathcal{S}}_{P;xy}\oplus\mathbb{R}\tau_{y}
.

Then the new extra data consists of embeddings
\mathbb{T}^{P}\to\mathbb{T}^{\mathcal{S}}_{xy}
along each
N_{P;xy}\times P
such that the following diagrams commute over appropriate singular faces:

\textstyle{\mathbb{T}^{P}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathbb{T}^{\mathcal{S}}_{xx^{\prime}y}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathbb{T}^{\mathcal{S}}_{xy}}

\textstyle{\mathbb{T}^{P}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathbb{T}^{\mathcal{S}}_{xy^{\prime}y}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathbb{T}^{\mathcal{S}}_{xy}}

along with stable isomorphisms

st^{N^{\mathcal{S}}}_{P;xy}:V_{x}\oplus I^{N^{\mathcal{S}}}_{P;xy}\oplus\mathbb{R}\sigma_{P}\to\mathbb{R}^{i}\oplus V_{y}-\mathbb{R}^{k}

with stabilising bundles the orthogonal complements of
\mathbb{T}^{P}
in
\mathbb{T}^{\mathcal{S}}_{xy}
(where
i=1
(resp.
i=2
)), such that the following diagram commutes over each
N^{\mathcal{S}}_{P;xy}\times P
:

\textstyle{V_{x}\oplus I^{N^{\mathcal{S}}}_{P;xy}\oplus\mathbb{R}\sigma_{P}\oplus TP\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{st^{N^{\mathcal{S}}}_{P;xy}\oplus st^{P}}
\scriptstyle{\psi}
\textstyle{\mathbb{R}^{i}\oplus V_{y}-\mathbb{R}^{k}\oplus\mathbb{R}^{k}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{V_{x}\oplus I^{\mathcal{S}}_{xy}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{st^{\mathcal{S}}}
\textstyle{\mathbb{R}^{i}\oplus V_{y}}

where
i=1
(resp.
i=2
) and the right vertical arrow uses the natural stable isomorphism
-\mathbb{R}^{k}\oplus\mathbb{R}^{k}\to 0
.

Note that this definition implies that the following diagrams commute. Over a broken face
\mathcal{S}_{xx^{\prime};y}\subseteq\mathcal{S}_{xy}
:

\textstyle{V_{x}\oplus I^{\mathcal{F}}_{xx^{\prime}}\oplus I^{N^{\mathcal{S}}}_{P;x^{\prime}y}\oplus\mathbb{R}\sigma_{P}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{st^{\mathcal{F}}}
\scriptstyle{\psi}
\textstyle{V_{x^{\prime}}\oplus I^{N^{\mathcal{S}}}_{P;x^{\prime}y}\oplus\mathbb{R}\sigma_{P}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{st^{N^{\mathcal{S}}}}
\textstyle{V_{x}\oplus I^{N^{\mathcal{S}}}_{P;xy}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{st^{N^{\mathcal{S}}}}
\textstyle{\mathbb{R}^{i}\oplus V_{y}-\mathbb{R}^{k}}

after extending along along the relevant embedding of stabilising bundle (where
i=1
(resp.
i=2
)) as well as a similar diagram over each
\mathcal{S}_{x;y^{\prime}y}
.

If
\mathcal{S}
is a bordism, then over an unbroken face
\mathcal{W}_{xy}\subseteq\mathcal{S}_{xy}
:

\textstyle{V_{x}\oplus I^{N^{\mathcal{S}}}_{xy}\oplus\mathbb{R}\sigma^{\mathcal{W}}\oplus\mathbb{R}\sigma_{P}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\psi}
\scriptstyle{st^{N^{\mathcal{W}}}}
\textstyle{\mathbb{R}\oplus\mathbb{R}\sigma^{\mathcal{W}}\oplus V_{y}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{=}
\textstyle{V_{x}\oplus I^{N^{\mathcal{S}}}_{xy}\oplus\mathbb{R}\sigma_{P}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{st^{N^{\mathcal{S}}}}
\textstyle{\mathbb{R}^{2}\oplus V_{y}}

Note that if
W
is a framed manifold with
\kBS
singularities, and
(f,s)
a framed function on
W
, then since
f
and
s
are constant in the
P
-direction of any singular face, the diagram in Definition
124
restricts directly to a corresponding diagram involving
T(f^{-1}(1))
and
T(f|_{N\times P})^{-1}(1)=T((f|_{N})^{-1}(1)\times P)
; thus we needn’t impose any further condition for
(f,s)
to be compatible with the framing.

Now all of the results and definitions in Sections
4
and
5
hold for morphisms and bordisms with
\kBS
-singularities (possibly with framings), as long as one truncates appropriately, i.e. works with
\tau_{n}
-morphisms everywhere where
2k-1>n
, with identical proofs. We illustrate this in the following case.

Let
\mathcal{W}:\mathcal{F}\to\mathcal{G}
and
\mathcal{V}:\mathcal{G}\to\mathcal{H}
be framed
\kBS
-pre-
\tau_{\leq n}
morphisms, with
2k>n
. We define
\tilde{\mathcal{Q}}_{xz}=\tilde{\mathcal{Q}}_{xz}(\mathcal{W},\mathcal{V})
as in Equation (
5
). We first analyse the structure of
\tilde{\mathcal{Q}}_{xz}
as a
\kBS
-manifold with faces more closely.

For each
x\in\mathcal{F}
,
y\in\mathcal{G}
and
z\in\mathcal{H}
with
|x|-|z|\leq n
, for dimension reasons at most one of
N^{\mathcal{W}}_{P;xy}
and
N^{\mathcal{V}}_{P;yz}
can be non-empty. If
N^{\mathcal{W}}_{P;xy}
is non-empty, we let
N^{\mathcal{W}\times\mathcal{V}}_{P;xyz}=N^{\mathcal{W}}_{P;xy}\times\mathcal{V}_{yz}
, if
N^{\mathcal{V}}_{P;yz}
is non-empty we let
N^{\mathcal{W}\times\mathcal{V}}_{P;xyz}=\mathcal{W}_{xy}\times N^{\mathcal{V}}_{P;yz}
, and if neither is non-empty we set
N^{\mathcal{W}\times\mathcal{V}}_{P;xyz}
to also be empty. Similarly we let
N^{\mathcal{W}\times\mathcal{V}}_{P;xyy^{\prime}z}
be
N^{\mathcal{W}}_{P;xy}\times\mathcal{V}_{yy^{\prime};z}
or
\mathcal{W}_{x;yy^{\prime}}\times N^{\mathcal{V}}_{P;y^{\prime}z}
(or empty) as appropriate. Then each
\mathcal{W}_{xy}\times\mathcal{V}_{yz}
is a
\kBS
-manifold with faces, with singular boundary faces given by the
N^{\mathcal{W}\times\mathcal{V}}_{P;xyz}\times P
.

Let
N^{\tilde{\mathcal{Q}}}_{P;xz}
be the coequaliser of the following diagram

\bigsqcup\limits_{y,y^{\prime}\in\mathcal{G}}N^{\mathcal{W}\times\mathcal{V}}_{P;xyy^{\prime}z}\times[0,\varepsilon)^{2}\rightrightarrows\bigsqcup\limits_{y\in\mathcal{G}}N^{\mathcal{W}\times\mathcal{V}}_{P;xyz}\times[0,\varepsilon)

where the maps are the restrictions of the maps in (
5
). Then
\tilde{\mathcal{Q}}_{xz}
is a
\kBS
-manifold with faces, with singular boundary faces given by the
N^{\tilde{\mathcal{Q}}}_{P;xz}
.

Choosing framed functions compatible on overlaps as in Section
4.1
lets us define the composition of
\mathcal{W}
and
\mathcal{V}
as unoriented
\kBS
-pre-
\tau_{\leq n}
-morphisms. To equip this composition with framings, we frame each
N^{\tilde{\mathcal{Q}}}_{P;xz}
by defining stable isomorphisms

st^{N^{\tilde{\mathcal{Q}}}}:V_{x}\oplus I^{N^{\tilde{\mathcal{Q}}}}_{P;xy}\oplus\mathbb{R}\sigma_{P}\to\mathbb{R}^{2}\oplus V_{y}-\mathbb{R}^{k}

as follows. We first choose, inductively in
|x|-|z|
, stabilising bundles
\mathbb{T}^{\tilde{\mathcal{Q}}}_{xz}
over each
\tilde{\mathcal{Q}}_{xz}
, along with embeddings of the stabilising bundle over each face (this is
\mathbb{T}^{P}
for some
P
over each singular face, and as in Lemma
70
over each essential face) into
\mathbb{T}^{\tilde{\mathcal{Q}}}_{xz}
which are compatible on overlaps.

Over each
N^{\mathcal{W}}_{P;xy}\times\mathcal{V}_{yz}\times[0,\varepsilon)
, we define
st^{N^{\tilde{\mathcal{Q}}}}
to be the following composition:

V_{x}\oplus I^{N^{\tilde{\mathcal{Q}}}}_{P;xz}\oplus\mathbb{R}\sigma_{P}\xrightarrow{\psi^{-1}}V_{x}\oplus I^{N^{\mathcal{W}}}_{P;xy}\oplus I^{\mathcal{V}}_{yz}\oplus\mathbb{R}\sigma_{P}\xrightarrow{st^{\mathcal{V}}\circ st^{N^{\mathcal{W}}}}\mathbb{R}\oplus\mathbb{R}\oplus V_{z}-\mathbb{R}^{k}

extended to have stabilising bundle
\mathbb{T}^{\tilde{\mathcal{Q}}}_{xz}
, where the first copy of
\mathbb{R}
on the target comes from
st^{N^{\mathcal{W}}}
and the second from
st^{\mathcal{V}}
.

These glue together to compatibly frame each
\tilde{\mathcal{Q}}_{xz}
compatibly with the singularities, just as in Lemma
70
; we then frame the composed morphism in the usual way.

In particular, this shows there is a category
\Flow^{fr}_{\tau_{\leq n},\kBS}
whenever
2k-1>n
with objects framed flow categories; we write morphisms in this category as
[\cdot,\cdot]^{fr}_{\tau_{\leq n},\kBS}
. We may also compose morphisms with singularities with non-singular bilinear maps in exactly the same way.

From Propositions
125
and
126
, it follows that

[*[i],*]^{fr}_{\tau_{\leq n},{(n-1)}\mathrm{-BS}}\simeq\begin{cases}\Omega^{fr}_{i}&\textrm{ if }i\leq n-2\\
0&\textrm{ if }i=n-1\\
\Omega^{fr,(n-1)\textrm{-BS}}_{n}\textrm{, which }\Omega^{fr}_{n-1}\cdot\Omega^{fr}_{1}\textrm{ dies in}&\textrm{ if }i=n\\
0&\textrm{ if }i>n\end{cases}

7
Spectral Donaldson-Fukaya category

In this section we prove Theorem
1
, by defining a unital associative category
\scrF(X;\mathbb{S})
enriched in graded abelian groups, which we call a
spectral Donaldson-Fukaya category
, and furthermore give the proof of Theorem
2
. The morphism spaces of
\scrF(X;\mathbb{S})
are modules over the framed bordism ring
\Omega_{*}^{fr}(\ast)
of a point. Note however that any direct interpretation in terms of the category of spectra is at this point conjectural.

The category
\scrF(X;\mathbb{S})
is defined when
X
admits a stable trivialisation of
TX
, as a complex vector bundle, i.e. for some
k
we have:

\Psi:TX\oplus\mathbb{C}^{k}\xrightarrow{\simeq}\mathbb{C}^{n+k}

(12)

The construction of
\scrF(X;\mathbb{S})
appeals to the existence of smooth structures on the relevant moduli spaces of holomorphic curves, and of stable framings of those moduli spaces, due to Fukaya, Oh, Ohta and Ono and Large. This material is subsequently reviewed in Section
8
.

7.1
Objects

Suppose
X
is stably framed, with a fixed trivialisation (
12
).
Consider a closed exact Lagrangian
L\subseteq X
, equipped with a nullhomotopy
h
of the stable Gauss map determined by
\Psi
:

\Psi_{L}:L\rightarrow U(n+k)/O(n+k)\rightarrow U/O

where the first arrow sends
p
in
L
to the totally real subspace
\Psi(T_{p}L\oplus\mathbb{R}^{k})\subseteq\mathbb{C}^{n+k}
and the second map is the stabilisation map.

Lemma 128
.

Given
L
as above, the nullhomotopy
h
of the stable trivialisation
\Psi_{L}
defines a brane structure on
L
, in the sense of
[
Sei08
]
, canonical up to an even shift.

Proof 7.1
.

The isomorphism
\psi:TX\oplus\mathbb{C}^{k}\to\mathbb{C}^{n+k}
gives a preferred trivialisation of
\Lambda^{top}_{\mathbb{C}}(TX)
and hence a preferred volume form
\eta
with associated phase function

\alpha:\mathrm{Gr}_{\mathrm{Lag}}(TX)\to S^{1}.

Since a stabilisation of
\psi_{L}
is null,
there is some
K>k
for which the map
x\mapsto\psi_{L}(T_{p}L\oplus\mathbb{R}^{K})
is homotopically trivial
L\to U(n+K)/O(n+K)
, which implies that the induced map
\alpha:L\to S^{1}
is homotopic to a constant map. It therefore admits a lift to
\mathbb{R}
; since the phase function is defined by a volume form and not a quadratic volume form, this is well-defined up to translation by an even integer (equivalently, the stable framing canonically orients the Lagrangian).
Finally, the stable framing
\psi_{L}
induces a spin structure on
L
. This completes the brane data in the sense of
[
Sei08
]
.

Definition 129
.

A
spectral Lagrangian brane
is a triple
(L,h,\eta)
where
L
is a Lagrangian submanifold with stable framing
\Psi_{L}
,
h
is a nullhomotopy of
\Psi_{L}
and
\eta
is an associated grading.

The objects of
\scrF(X;\mathbb{S})
are defined to be spectral Lagrangian branes. Given a spectral Lagrangian brane
(L,h,\eta)
, we will often drop
\{h,\eta\}
from the notation.

7.2
Morphisms

For every pair
L
,
K
of spectral Lagrangian branes, we choose regular Floer data
(H_{t},J_{t})
, for
t
in
[0,1]
, as in
[
Lar
, Section 5.2]
. We define a framed flow category
\overline{\mathcal{M}}^{LK}
as follows.

The objects of
\overline{\mathcal{M}}^{LK}
are Hamiltonian chords from
L
to
K
, meaning maps
x:[0,1]\to X
, such that

1.

x(0)
lies in
L
.

2.

x(1)
lies in
K
.

3.

dH_{t}=\omega(\cdot,\dot{x}(t))

The grading
\overline{\mathcal{M}}^{LK}\to\mathbb{Z}
is given by the Conley-Zehnder index, as in
[
Sei08
]
.

For
x
,
y
in
\overline{\mathcal{M}}^{LK}
, we define the (uncompactified) moduli space
\mathcal{M}^{LK}_{xy}
to be the space of maps
u:\mathbb{R}_{s}\times[0,1]_{t}\to X
, such that

1.

u(\mathbb{R}\times\{0\})
lies in
L
.

2.

u(\mathbb{R}\times\{1\})
lies in
K
.

3.

u(-\infty,\cdot)\to x
and
u(+\infty,\cdot)\to y
exponentially fast in any
C^{k}
norm.

4.

\partial_{s}u+J_{t}(\partial_{t}u-X_{t}(u))=0

quotiented by the free
\mathbb{R}
-action by translation. For
x_{0},\ldots,x_{i}
in
\overline{\mathcal{M}}^{LK}
, we write
\mathcal{M}^{LK}_{x_{0}\ldots x_{i}}
for
\mathcal{M}^{LK}_{x_{0}x_{1}}\times\ldots\times\mathcal{M}^{LK}_{x_{i-1}x_{i}}
.

We define

\overline{\mathcal{M}}^{LK}_{xy}:=\bigsqcup\limits_{\begin{subarray}{c}i\geq 1\\
x_{0}=x,\ldots,x_{i}=y\in\overline{\mathcal{M}}^{LK}\end{subarray}}\mathcal{M}^{LK}_{x_{0}\ldots x_{i}}

equipped with the Gromov topology. The concatenation maps are given by inclusions of the compactification strata.

For each Hamiltonian chord
x
, we have a stable vector space
V(x)
as constructed in Section
8
coming as a fibre of the index bundle over a space of abstract Floer caps for the chord. The linearisations of the
\overline{\partial}
-operators on Floer solutions assemble to define an index bundle
\mathrm{Ind}_{xy}\to\overline{\mathcal{M}}^{LK}_{xy}
, which glue associatively over boundary faces.

Theorem 130
.

The
\overline{\mathcal{M}}^{LK}_{xy}
admit the structures of compact smooth manifolds with corners, such that
\overline{\mathcal{M}}^{LK}
forms a framed flow category. Furthermore, the stable vector spaces
V_{x}
satisfy
I(x,y)\oplus V(y)\simeq V(x)
compatibly with gluing stable isomorphisms, hence define a framing on this flow category.

Proof 7.2
.

The first statement follows from Theorem
157
. The second result follows from Propositions
161
and
163
.

Definition 131
.

For each
L,K
, we define the morphisms from
L
to
K
of degree
i
, written
\scrF_{i}(L,K)
, to be

[*[i],\overline{\mathcal{M}}^{LK}]^{fr}
.

Lemma 132
.

The morphism groups of
\scrF(X;\mathbb{S})
are naturally graded modules over
\Omega^{fr}_{*}(\ast)
, so there is a homomorphism

\Omega^{fr}_{i}\otimes\scrF_{j}(L,K)\longrightarrow\scrF_{i+j}(L,K).

Proof 7.3
.

Given a framed flow module
\mathcal{W}
, i.e. a framed morphism of flow categories
\ast
to
\overline{\mathcal{M}}^{LK}
, and a closed framed manifold
P
, we obtain another framed flow module by multiplying all the manifolds with faces in
\mathcal{W}
by
P
. It is straightforward that this operation descends to framed bordism classes.

7.3
Composition

For each triple
L
,
K
,
M
of spectral Lagrangian branes, we define a bilinear map

\overline{\mathcal{M}}^{LKM}:\overline{\mathcal{M}}^{KM}\times\overline{\mathcal{M}}^{LK}\to\overline{\mathcal{M}}^{LM}

as follows.

We choose three boundary marked points
\zeta_{0}^{+},\zeta_{1}^{-},\zeta_{2}^{-}
on the disc
D^{2}
, labelled anticlockwise. We label the components of
\partial D^{2}\setminus\{\zeta_{i}^{\pm}\}_{i}
by
L,K,M
respectively, ordered clockwise, so that
\zeta^{+}_{0}
is between the components labelled by
L
and
M
.

We choose strip-like ends
\varepsilon^{+}_{0},\varepsilon_{1}^{-},\varepsilon_{2}^{-}
near
\zeta^{+}_{0},\zeta^{-}_{1},\zeta^{-}_{2}
respectively, with domains depending on the sign. This means
\varepsilon^{\pm}_{i}:\mathbb{R}_{\pm}\times[0,1]\hookrightarrow D^{2}
parametrises a holomorphic punctured neighbourhood of
\zeta^{\pm}_{i}
. We choose these neighbourhoods to be disjoint.

We choose regular perturbation data
(K,J_{z})
, where
J_{z}
is an appropriate almost complex structure for
z
in
D^{2}\setminus\{\zeta^{\pm}_{i}\}_{i}
, and
K
is a 1-form in
\Omega^{1}(D^{2}\setminus\{\zeta^{\pm}_{i}\}_{i},C^{\infty}(X,\mathbb{R}))
, as in
[
Lar
, Page 64]
. These are in particular chosen to be appropriately compatible with the Floer data chosen for each pair of Lagrangians in Section
7.2
.
K
determines a 1-form
Y
in
\Omega^{1}(D^{2}\setminus\{\zeta^{\pm}_{i}\}_{i},C^{\infty}(X,TX))
, as in
[
Sei08
, Page 105]
.

For
x
in
\overline{\mathcal{M}}^{LK}
,
y
in
\overline{\mathcal{M}}^{KM}
and
z
in
\overline{\mathcal{M}}^{LM}
, we define the (uncompactified) moduli space
{\mathcal{M}}^{LKM}_{yx;z}
to be the space of maps
u:D^{2}\setminus\{\zeta_{i}^{\pm}\}_{i}\to X
, such that

1.

u
sends each component of
\partial D^{2}\setminus\{\zeta^{\pm}_{i}\}_{i}
to the Lagrangian it is labelled by.

2.

u(\varepsilon^{\pm}_{i}(\pm\infty,\cdot))\to z,y\,\mathrm{\,}x
exponentially fast in any
C^{k}
norm, for
i=0,1\,\mathrm{or}\,2
, respectively.

3.

(du-Y)^{0,1}=0

We define

\overline{\mathcal{M}}^{LKM}_{yx;z}:=\bigsqcup\limits_{\begin{subarray}{c}i,j,k\geq 1\\
x_{0}=x,\ldots,x_{i}\in\overline{\mathcal{M}}^{LK}\\
y_{0}=y,\ldots,y_{j}\in\overline{\mathcal{M}}^{KM}\\
z_{0},\ldots,z_{k}=z\in\overline{\mathcal{M}}^{LM}\end{subarray}}\mathcal{M}^{LK}_{x_{0}\ldots x_{i}}\times\mathcal{M}^{KM}_{y_{0}\ldots y_{j}}\times\mathcal{M}^{LKM}_{y_{j}x_{i};z_{0}}\times\mathcal{M}^{LM}_{z_{0}\ldots z_{k}}

to be the stable map compactification,
equipped with the Gromov topology.

Theorem 133
.

The
\overline{\mathcal{M}}^{LKM}_{yx;z}
admit the structures of compact smooth manifolds with corners, for which there are gluing-compatible stable isomorphisms

V_{x}\oplus V_{y}\oplus I^{\overline{\mathcal{M}}^{LKM}}_{yx;z}\to V_{z}

such that
\overline{\mathcal{M}}^{LKM}
yields a framed bilinear map
\overline{\mathcal{M}}^{KM}\times\overline{\mathcal{M}}^{LK}\to\overline{\mathcal{M}}^{LM}
.

Proof 7.4
.

This follows from Propositions
159
and (the bilinear maps analogue of) Lemma
162
and Proposition
163
.

Definition 134
.

For each
L,K,M
, the composition map in
\scrF(X;\mathbb{S})
is given by

\overline{\mathcal{M}}^{LKM}_{*}:\scrF_{i}(K,M)\otimes\scrF_{j}(L,K)\to\scrF_{i+j}(L,M)

7.4
Associativity

For each quadruple
L,K,M,N
of spectral Lagrangian branes, we define an associator in order to show that composition for these objects is associative.

Let
\mathcal{R}^{\circ}=(-\infty,\infty)
. This has a family of Riemann surfaces with 4 boundary marked points living over it

\mathcal{S}\to\mathcal{R}
, defined as follows.

We define the fibre
\mathcal{R}_{r}
over
r
in
(-\infty,\infty)
to be the strip
\mathbb{R}\times[0,1]
, with marked points at
\pm\infty
,
0
and
(r,1)
.

We extend this to a family over
\mathcal{R}:=[-\infty,\infty]
by setting the fibres over
\pm\infty
to be appropriate nodal discs. Choosing charts
[-\infty,-1)\simeq(-1,0]
and
(1,\infty]\simeq[0,1)
via
r\mapsto\frac{1}{r}
in both cases endows
\mathcal{R}
with a smooth structure, and gluing at the nodes of the fibres over
\pm\infty
allows us to endow this family with the structure of a degenerating family of Riemann surfaces with marked points, as in
[
Lar
, Page 68]
.

We name the boundary marked points of each
\mathcal{S}_{r}
by
\zeta^{+}_{0},\zeta^{-}_{1},\zeta^{-}_{2},\zeta^{-}_{3}
, ordered anticlockwise. We label the components of each
\partial\mathcal{S}_{r}\setminus\{\zeta^{\pm}_{i}\}_{i}
by
L,K,M,N
respectively, ordered clockwise, so that
\zeta^{+}_{0}
is between the components labelled by
L
and
M
.

We choose disjoint strip-like ends
\varepsilon^{\pm}_{i}
near
\zeta^{\pm}_{i}
on each
\mathcal{S}_{r}
, along with strip-like ends near the nodes in each nodal fibre
\mathcal{S}_{\pm\infty}
, and we choose consistent perturbation data
(K_{r},Z_{z,r})
for each
r
in
\mathcal{R}
extending the Floer data chosen in Section
7.2
, and agreeing with the perturbation data chosen in Section
7.3
over the nodal fibres (see
[
Lar
, Page 68]
for further details).

For
x
in
\overline{\mathcal{M}}^{LK}
,
y
in
\overline{\mathcal{M}}^{KM}
,
z
in
\overline{\mathcal{M}}^{MN}
and
w
in
\overline{\mathcal{M}}^{LN}
, we define the (uncompactified) moduli space

{\mathcal{M}}^{LKMN}_{zyx;w}
to be the space of pairs
(u,r)
, where
u:\mathcal{S}_{r}\setminus\{\zeta^{\pm}_{i}\}_{i}\to X
, such that

1.

u
sends each component of
\partial\mathcal{S}_{r}\setminus\{\zeta^{\pm}_{i}\}_{i}
to the Lagrangian it is labelled by.

2.

u(\varepsilon^{\pm}_{i}(\pm,\cdot)\to z,y,x,w)
exponentially fast in any
C^{k}
norm, for
i=0,1,2\,\mathrm{or}\,3
respectively.

3.

(du-Y)^{0,1}=0

We define the compactification

\overline{\mathcal{M}}^{LKMN}_{zyx;w}:=\bigsqcup\limits_{\begin{subarray}{c}i,j,k,l\geq 1\\
x_{0}=x,\ldots,x_{i}\in\overline{\mathcal{M}}^{LK}\\
y_{0}=y,\ldots,y_{j}\in\overline{\mathcal{M}}^{KM}\\
z_{0}=z,\ldots,z_{k}\in\overline{\mathcal{M}}^{MN}\\
w_{0},\ldots,w_{l}=w\in\overline{\mathcal{M}}^{LN}\end{subarray}}\mathcal{M}^{LK}_{x_{0}\ldots x_{i}}\times\mathcal{M}^{KM}_{y_{0}\ldots y_{j}}\times\mathcal{M}^{MN}_{z_{0}\ldots z_{k}}\times\mathcal{M}^{LKMN}_{z_{k}y_{j}x_{i};w_{0}}\times\mathcal{M}^{LN}_{w_{0}\ldots w_{l}}

equipped with the Gromov topology.

Theorem 135
.

The
\overline{\mathcal{M}}^{LKMN}_{zyx;w}
admit the structures of compact smooth manifolds with corners, such that
\overline{\mathcal{M}}^{LKMN}
forms an associator for the tuple

(\overline{\mathcal{M}}^{MN},\overline{\mathcal{M}}^{KM},\overline{\mathcal{M}}^{LK},\overline{\mathcal{M}}^{KN},\overline{\mathcal{M}}^{LM},\overline{\mathcal{M}}^{LN},\overline{\mathcal{M}}^{KMN},\overline{\mathcal{M}}^{LKM},\overline{\mathcal{M}}^{LKN},\overline{\mathcal{M}}^{LKN},\overline{\mathcal{M}}^{LMN})

which admits the structure of a framed associator.

From Theorem
135
and Lemma
84
, it follows that

Corollary 136
.

The category
\scrF(X;\mathbb{S})
is associative.

7.5
Units

Let
L
be a spectral Lagrangian brane. Choosing appropriate Floer data on a disc with a single marked point, Theorem
157
gives us a morphism of framed flow categories
\overline{\mathcal{M}}^{L}:*\rightarrow\overline{\mathcal{M}}^{LL}
, which determines a class
[\overline{\mathcal{M}}^{L}]
in
\scrF_{0}(L,L)
.

Lemma 137
.

\overline{\mathcal{M}}^{L}
is idempotent.

Proof 7.5
.

Gluing the disc with one marked point on the boundary to a disc with three marked points on the boundary (and using Theorem
157
and Proposition
159
) shows that the morphism of flow categories
\overline{\mathcal{M}}^{LLL}\circ\overline{\mathcal{M}}^{L}
is framed bordant to the morphism of flow categories obtained by a continuation map from
\overline{\mathcal{M}}^{LL}
to itself. The same gluing argument as in
[
Sei08
]
, but also appealing to the smooth structures mentioned previously, implies that this morphism of flow categories is idempotent up to framed bordism.

Lemma 138
.

Post-composition with
\overline{\mathcal{M}}^{L}
is surjective.

More precisely, for any other brane
K
, the linear map

\overline{\mathcal{M}}^{KLL}_{*}(\cdot,[\overline{\mathcal{M}}^{L}]):\scrF_{*}(K,L)\rightarrow\scrF_{*}(K,L)

is surjective.

Proof 7.6
.

A gluing argument using Proposition
159
shows that
[\overline{\mathcal{M}}^{KLL}\circ\overline{\mathcal{M}}^{L}]:\overline{\mathcal{M}}^{KL}\rightarrow\overline{\mathcal{M}}^{KL}
can be represented by a continuation morphism coming from a homotopy of Floer data. By
[
Sei08
]
these induce isomorphisms in the Fukaya category over any field and hence in the integral Fukaya category, so
[\tau_{\leq 0}(\overline{\mathcal{M}}^{KLL}\circ\overline{\mathcal{M}}^{L})]
is an isomorphism in
\Flow_{\tau_{\leq 0}}
. The result follows by Theorem
111
.

Corollary 139
.

\scrF(X;\mathbb{S})
is a unital category, where the unit for each
L
is
[\overline{\mathcal{M}}^{L}]
.

Proof 7.7
.

Choose a class
a
in
\scrF_{*}(L,K)
. By the previous result, there is some class
b
with
a=b\circ[\overline{\mathcal{M}}^{L}]
. By Lemma
137
,

a=b\circ[\overline{\mathcal{M}}^{L}]=b\circ[\overline{\mathcal{M}}^{L}]\circ[\overline{\mathcal{M}}^{L}]=a\circ[\overline{\mathcal{M}}^{L}]

A similar argument shows
[\overline{\mathcal{M}}^{K}]\circ a=a
.

Corollary 140
.

An element
x\in\scrF(L,L)
is invertible exactly when
\tau_{\leq 0}(x)\in CF(L,L;\mathbb{Z})
is invertible.

Proof 7.8
.

One direction is straightforward. The other follows from Theorem
111
, which implies that if
a\in\scrF_{i}(L,L)=[\ast[i],\overline{\mathcal{M}}^{LL}]^{fr}
and composition with
\tau_{\leq 0}a
induces a quasi-isomorphism on
CF(L,L;\mathbb{Z})
then composition with
a
is surjective. In particular the identity
[\overline{\mathcal{M}}^{L}]
then lies in the image of composition with
a
so
a
is invertible.

7.6
Open-closed maps

Let
\mathcal{F}
and
\mathcal{G}
be flow categories and
\mathcal{W}:\mathcal{F}\to\mathcal{G}
be a morphism. Fix a background manifold
X
. We say that
\mathcal{W}

lives over
X
(or is ‘equipped with an
X
-valued local system’) if there are smooth evaluation maps

\ev_{xy}^{X}:\mathcal{W}_{xy}\to X

which are compatible with breaking, meaning that there are commutative diagrams

\textstyle{\mathcal{F}_{xy}\times\mathcal{W}_{yz}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\ev_{yz}^{X}\circ(\mathrm{proj})}
\textstyle{\mathcal{W}_{xz}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\ev_{xz}^{X}}
\textstyle{X\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{X}

and

\textstyle{\mathcal{W}_{xy}\times\mathcal{G}_{yz}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\ev_{xy}^{X}\circ(\mathrm{proj})}
\textstyle{\mathcal{W}_{xz}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\ev_{xz}^{X}}
\textstyle{X\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{X}

where
(\mathrm{proj})
denotes projection to the
\mathcal{W}
-factor of the product.
There is an analogous notion of a bilinear map
\mathcal{W}:\mathcal{F}\times\mathcal{G}\to\mathcal{H}
of flow categories living over
X
(i.e. equipped with evaluation maps to
X
compatibly with all breaking).

A
homotopy

\mathcal{R}
between bilinear maps
\mathcal{W}^{i}:\mathcal{F}\times\mathcal{G}\to\mathcal{H}
, with
i=0,1
, comprises manifolds with faces
\mathcal{R}_{xy;z}
of dimension
|x|+|y|-|z|+1
and living over
[0,1]
, together with identifications

\ev^{-1}(0)=\mathcal{W}^{0}_{xy;z}\quad\ev^{-1}(1)=\mathcal{W}^{1}_{xy;z}.

(13)

If
\mathcal{W}^{0}
and
\mathcal{W}^{1}
live over
X
then the homotopy lives over
X
if there are evaluation maps
\mathcal{R}_{xy;z}\to X
compatible with breaking and also with the identifications of (
13
).

The above definitions carry over unchanged to the case of framed morphisms of framed flow categories, and framed homotopies of framed bilinear maps (the evaluation maps do not need to be compatible with any particular data on the target manifold
X
).

Recall that if
L
is a spectral Lagrangian brane then the unit
[\overline{\mathcal{M}}^{L}]
is defined via a morphism
e:*\to\overline{\mathcal{M}}^{LL}
which counts holomorphic discs with one boundary puncture (viewed as an output, at which we turn on a Hamiltonian term so the output is a chord). Choosing a negative rather than positive strip-like end at the puncture, we also obtain a morphism

\Phi^{L}:\overline{\mathcal{M}}^{LL}\to\ast[-n].

Now fix an interior marked point in the disc. Evaluation at that point shows that the morphism
\Phi
lives over
X
. The composition

\mathcal{C}:\Phi^{L}\circ e:*\to*[-n]

comprises a single closed framed manifold
\mathcal{C}_{**}
of dimension
n
, which inherits a map to
X
.

Lemma 141
.

The manifold
\mathcal{C}_{**}
is framed bordant over
X
to the inclusion
L\hookrightarrow X
.

Proof 7.9
.

Gluing shows that the composition
\Phi_{L}\circ e
is given by a framed morphism
*\to*[-n]
of flow categories for which the unique moduli space is the space of perturbed holomorphic discs with no boundary punctures and with boundary on
L
. Turning off the Hamiltonian term in the Floer equation, this morphism is framed bordant to one in which all such discs are constant by exactness.

Now consider spectral branes
L,K
. We consider Floer data on the infinite strip
\mathbb{R}\times[0,1]
with Lagrangian boundary conditions
L,K
and we fix an additional boundary marked point. Depending on which boundary component the marked point lies on, we obtain two bilinear maps

\mathcal{W}^{L}:\overline{\mathcal{M}}^{LK}\times\overline{\mathcal{M}}^{KL}\to\ast\qquad\textrm{and}\qquad\mathcal{W}^{K}:\overline{\mathcal{M}}^{LK}\times\overline{\mathcal{M}}^{KL}\to\ast.

(14)

As usual these admit the structure of framed bilinear maps.

Lemma 142
.

The bilinear maps from (
14
) are homotopic over
X
.

Proof 7.10
.

The bilinear maps live over
X
by evaluation at the boundary marked point. A homotopy of the given bilinear maps over
X
is obtained by considering the Gromov-compactified moduli spaces of strips which have a single marked point which lies somewhere on a fixed path
\gamma(t)
between the two boundary components (say
\{0\}\times[0,1]\subset\mathbb{R}\times[0,1]
); cf. Figure
2
. The moduli spaces defining the homotopy live over
[0,1]
by recording the point
t
on the path specifying the conformal structure of the domain, and the faces lying over
0,1
are clearly those associated to
\mathcal{W}^{K}
and
\mathcal{W}^{L}
.

To relate the preceding two constructions, recall that we have a bilinear composition

\mu^{2}_{KLK}:\scrF_{i}(L,K)\otimes\scrF_{j}(K,L)\to\scrF_{i+j}(K,K).

Lemma 143
.

\Phi^{K}\circ\mu^{2}_{KLK}(x,y)=\mathcal{W}^{K}(x,y)
, and similarly for
L
.

Proof 7.11
.

This follows from the gluing argument illustrated in Figure
4
.

x
y
K
L
K
\Phi_{K}
K
K
L
x
y

Figure 4:
Gluing relates a bilinear map to the product composed with the unit

Corollary 144
.

If spectral Lagrangian branes
L
and
K
are isomorphic in
\scrF(X;\mathbb{S})
then they are framed bordant over
X
. In particular they define the same class in
\Omega_{n}^{fr}(X)
.

Proof 7.12
.

This follows from combining Lemma
141
with Lemma
143
.

{rmk}

A choice of Morse function
f:X\to\mathbb{R}
defines a framed flow category
\overline{\mathcal{M}}^{f}
as in Example
4.1
(see
[
Weh12
]
for a proof of smoothness and
[
CJS95
]
for the framings). Defining
CM_{i}(X;\Omega^{fr}):=[*[i],\overline{\mathcal{M}}^{f}]^{fr}
, there are then natural morphisms
\mathcal{OC}_{L}:\scrF_{i}(L,L)\to CM_{i}(X;\Omega^{fr})
obtained from taking fibre products of the moduli spaces defining
[\overline{\mathcal{M}}^{L}]
with unstable manifolds of critical points of
f
, where we evaluate the punctured discs at a fixed interior point. One can reformulate the previous argument in terms of these more classical open-closed maps (and their length two cousin), but the chosen formulation avoids the need to relate Morse theory on
X
to
X
itself.

7.7
Floer theory with Baas-Sullivan singularities

Assume
2k>n+1
. We define a category
\tau_{\leq n}\scrF(X;\mathbb{S})_{\kBS}
whose objects are spectral Lagrangian branes, but whose morphism groups are given by the groups

[\ast[i],\overline{\mathcal{M}}^{LK}]_{\tau\leq n,\kBS}^{fr}

An associative composition then arises from Section
6.3
, using that the spaces of holomorphic triangles and discs with four marked points gave a framed bilinear map and corresponding framed associator. Analogous to Corollary
144
we have

Corollary 145
.

If
L
and
K
are spectral Lagrangian branes which are quasi-isomorphic in
\tau_{\leq n}\scrF(X;\mathbb{S})_{\kBS}
then their fundamental classes agree in
\Omega_{n}^{fr;\kBS}(X)
.

7.8
Comparisons

We assume as usual that we have fixed a stable trivialisation of
TX
, and that
L
and
K
are spectral Lagrangian branes. Since these carry gradings, a choice of regular Floer data
(H_{t},J_{t})
for
(L,K)
gives rise to a ‘classical’ Floer cochain complex
CF^{*}(L,K)
as constructed in
[
Sei08
]
, as well as to the Floer flow category
\overline{\mathcal{M}}^{LK}
.

Lemma 146
.

There is an identification of complexes

CM_{*}(\overline{\mathcal{M}}^{LK})=CF^{-*}(L,K).

Proof 7.13
.

Basically tautological. Note that our grading conventions are homological but still with a degree zero unit and graded product, which ensures that
CM_{i}=CF^{-i}
.

Lemma 147
.

Under the equivalence of Lemma
146
, the product induced by the framed bilinear map
\overline{\mathcal{M}}^{LKM}
in Theorem
133
agrees with the Floer triangle product.

Proof 7.14
.

Clear.

Lemma 148
.

Suppose
2k>n+1
. There are forgetful functors

\scrF(X;\mathbb{S})\to\tau_{\leq n}\scrF(X;\mathbb{S})\to\tau_{\leq n}\scrF(X;\mathbb{S})_{\kBS}.

Moreover, when
n=0
, there is an embedding

\tau_{\leq 0}\scrF(X;\mathbb{S})\hookrightarrow\scrF(X;\mathbb{Z})

with image the full subcategory of exact Lagrangians which admit some spectral brane structure.

Proof 7.15
.

The forgetful functors are clear. That morphisms in the
\tau\leq 0
-Fukaya category agree with morphisms in the integral Fukaya category follows from Lemma
146
, and that that identification is multiplicative follows from Lemma
147
.

7.9
Proof of Theorem
2

We recall the statement:

Theorem 149
.

Let
X
be a Liouville manifold with
TX
stably trivial as a complex vector bundle, and fix a stable trivialisation
\phi
. Let
L,K\subset X
be closed exact Lagrangian integer homology spheres whose stable Gauss maps are stably trivial compatibly with
\phi
. If
L
and
K
admit gradings such that they represent quasi-isomorphic objects of
\scrF(X;\mathbb{Z})
then they represent the same class in the quotient
\Omega_{n}^{fr}(X)/(\Omega_{1}^{fr}(X)\cdot\Omega_{n-1})=\Omega_{n}^{fr;(n-1)\mathrm{-BS}}(X)
. In particular, by Proposition
123
,
[L]
and
[K]
agree in
\Omega_{n}^{fr}(\ast)/\mathrm{image}(\eta)
.

The hypotheses imply that there is a spectral Donaldson-Fukaya category
\scrF(X;\mathbb{S})
which is unital and associative, and which contains objects
L
and
K
whose brane structures lift classical gradings. We assume those gradings are chosen so that
L
and
K
are quasi-isomorphic in
\scrF(X;\mathbb{Z})=\tau_{\leq 0}\scrF(X;\mathbb{S})
.

Let
\alpha\in\tau_{\leq 0}\scrF_{n}(L,K)
and
\beta\in\tau_{\leq 0}\scrF_{n}(K,L)
be inverse quasi-isomorphisms (with homological grading, so the product maps
CF_{i}\otimes CF_{j}\to CF_{i+j-n}
).

Lemma 150
.

\alpha
and
\beta
admit lifts
\tilde{\alpha}\in\scrF_{n}(L,K;\Omega^{fr;(n-1)-BS})
and
\tilde{\beta}\in\scrF_{n}(K,L;\Omega^{fr;(n-1)-BS})
respectively.

Proof 7.16
.

According to (the framed version of) Theorem
109
the obstruction to lifting
\alpha
to a
\tau_{\leq 1}
-morphism lies in

\Hom(CM_{*+2}(\ast[n]),CM_{*}(\overline{\mathcal{M}}^{LK};\Omega_{1}^{fr}))

The domain vanishes unless
*=n-2
, in which case the target vanishes using that
HF_{n-2}(L,K)=0
. Iteratively, for degree reasons (using that
L
and
K
are integer homology spheres), the unique obstruction comes when lifting from a
\tau_{\leq n-2}
to a
\tau_{\leq n-1}
morphism, and is valued in
HM_{0}(\overline{\mathcal{M}}^{LK},\Omega_{n-1}^{fr})
. This vanishes in
\Omega^{fr;(n-1)-BS}_{n-1}
.

Lemma 151
.

The compositions
\tilde{\alpha}\circ\tilde{\beta}
and
\tilde{\beta}\circ\tilde{\alpha}
are both units.

Proof 7.17
.

This follows from Corollary
140
.

Lemma 152
.

There are inverse quasi-equivalences

\tilde{\alpha}^{\prime}\in\scrF_{n}(L,K;\Omega^{fr;(n-1)-BS})

and

\tilde{\beta}^{\prime}\in\scrF_{n}(K,L;\Omega^{fr;(n-1)-BS}).

Proof 7.18
.

Let

\tilde{\beta}^{\prime}=\tilde{\beta}(\tilde{\alpha}\tilde{\beta})^{-1}.

Then
\tilde{\alpha}\tilde{\beta}^{\prime}=\id
, but also if
\gamma:=\tilde{\beta}^{\prime}\tilde{\alpha}
then
\gamma
is idempotent. But

\gamma(1-\gamma)=0\Rightarrow\gamma=1

since
\gamma
is by construction a unit. Thus, we have found a quasi-inverse for
\tilde{\alpha}
, as required.

The conclusion of Theorem
2
now follows from Corollary
145
.

7.10
Endomorphisms

Though not required for the proof of Theorem
2
, in this section we identify the endomorphism groups in
\scrF
.

Proposition 153
.

Let
L
be a spectral Lagrangian brane.

Then there are isomorphisms of abelian groups

\scrF_{i-n}(L,L)\simeq\Omega^{fr}_{i}(L)

We provide a sketch proof, noting that to make the final step rigorous requires some Morse-Bott gluing.

Proof 7.19

(Sketch proof)
.

We consider the morphisms
\overline{\mathcal{M}}^{L}:*\to\overline{\mathcal{M}}^{LL}
and
\Phi^{L}:\overline{\mathcal{M}}^{LL}\to*[-n]
, which both live over
X
by evaluating at a choice of boundary marked points. By choosing the regular Floer data and the boundary marked point for
\overline{\mathcal{M}}^{L}
appropriately, i.e. choosing a boundary point away from the support of the Hamiltonian term, we can assume that the evaluation map lands in
L
(rather than some small Hamiltonian translate).

We define maps
\alpha:\Omega_{i}^{fr}(L)\to\scrF_{i-n}(L,L)
and
\beta:\scrF_{i-n}(L,L)\to\Omega_{i}^{fr}(L)
as follows.

Let
b=(s:M^{i}\to L,TM\simeq\mathbb{R}^{i})
represent a class in
\Omega_{i}^{fr}
; generically perturbing
s
if necessary lets us assume that it is transverse to all
\overline{\mathcal{M}}^{L}_{*x}
.

We define
\alpha(b)
to be represented by the morphism
*[i-n]\to\overline{\mathcal{M}}^{LL}
determined by the manifolds given by the (transverse) fibre products
M\times_{L}\overline{\mathcal{M}}^{L}_{*x}
; this acquires a natural framing from those on
M
and
\overline{\mathcal{M}}^{L}
. A similar fibre product construction applied to bordisms shows that this descends to a well-defined map
\alpha
.

Let
(\mathcal{A}:*[i-n]\to\mathcal{M}^{LL})
represent a class in
\scrF_{i-n}(L,L)
. Then we define
\beta(\mathcal{A})
to be represented by
(\Phi^{L}\circ\mathcal{A})_{*}*
, which lives over
L
via the evaluation maps of
\Phi^{L}
.

Then
\beta\circ\alpha
sends
b
as above to
M\times_{L}\mathcal{C}_{**}
, where
\mathcal{C}=\Phi^{L}\circ\overline{\mathcal{M}}^{L}
as in Section
7.6
, therefore by Lemma
141

\beta\circ\alpha
is the identity.

To see that
\alpha\circ\beta
is the identity, we observe that it sends
\mathcal{A}
to
\mathcal{A}\circ\mathcal{W}
, for the morphism
\mathcal{W}:\overline{\mathcal{M}}^{LL}\to\overline{\mathcal{M}}^{LL}
with
\mathcal{W}_{xy}=\Phi^{L}_{x*}\times_{L}\overline{\mathcal{M}}^{L}_{*y}
, generically perturbing so this is always a transverse fibre product; then a Morse-Bott argument shows that
\mathcal{W}
is bordant to a continuation map which is bordant to multiplication by the unit.

An identical argument, with
\overline{\mathcal{M}}^{LL}
replaced by the framed flow category
\overline{\mathcal{M}}^{f}
of a Morse function
f:M\to\mathbb{R}
on a closed manifold, and further replacing
\overline{\mathcal{M}}^{L}
and
\Phi^{L}
by the morphisms given by the compactified ascending and descending manifolds, shows that the groups
[*[i],\overline{\mathcal{M}}^{f}]^{fr}
agree with the framed bordism groups of
M
.

8
Floer flow category technicalities

8.1
Smooth structures on spaces of strips

We summarise some results of Large
[
Lar
, Sections 6,7]
, which build on work of Fukaya-Oh-Ohta-Ono
[
FOOOar
]
, on smooth structures on compactified moduli spaces of Floer solutions.

Let
L,K
be exact Lagrangians and fix Floer data
(H_{t},J_{t})
for which
L
and
\phi_{H_{t}}^{1}(K)
meet transversely, and moduli spaces of Floer solutions are cut out transversely. We write
\mathcal{W}_{xy}
for the moduli space of Floer solutions,
\mathcal{M}_{xy}
for its quotient by the translation reparametrization action and
\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{xy}
for the Gromov compactification of
\mathcal{M}_{xy}
.

Let
x_{0},\ldots,x_{n}
be Hamiltonian chords from
L
to the time-one image of
K
, and
let
u\in\mathcal{W}_{x_{0}x_{n}}
be a Floer strip. Recall that a point
z=(s,t)\in\mathbb{R}\times[0,1]
is ‘regular’ for
u
if

u^{-1}(u(\mathbb{R}\times\{t\})=z;\ \partial_{s}u|_{z}\neq 0;\ u(z)\neq x_{i}(t),\,i\in\{0,n\}.

(15)

[
FHS95
]
shows that the set of regular points is open and dense. Now suppose
u
has Maslov index
d+1
, so that
\mathcal{W}_{x_{0}x_{n}}
has dimension
d+1
and
\mathcal{M}_{x_{0},x_{n}}
has dimension
d
. Pick
z_{0},z_{1},\ldots,z_{d}
in
\mathbb{R}\times[0,1]
with the properties that they are all regular, and if
z_{i}=(s_{i},t_{i})
then
s_{0}<s_{1}<\ldots<s_{d}
.

Definition 154
.

A collection
\textbf{H}=\{H_{0},\ldots,H_{d}\}
of smooth codimension one open hypersurfaces of
X
form a
complete system
(of hypersurfaces) for
u
if the following two conditions hold:

1.

the
evaluation map
\mathcal{W}_{x_{0},x_{n}}\to X^{d+1}
sending
v\mapsto(v(z_{0}),v(z_{1}),\ldots,v(z_{d}))
is transverse to
H_{0}\times H_{1}\times\cdots\times H_{d}
at
u
;

2.

for each
0\leq i\leq d
,
u^{-1}(H_{i})
and
\mathbb{R}\times\{t_{i}\}
intersect transversely at
z_{i}
in
\mathbb{R}\times[0,1]
, and moreover
z_{i}
is the only intersection of
u^{-1}(\overline{H}_{i})
and
\mathbb{R}\times\{t_{i}\}
.

Lemma 155
.

Every
u
admits a complete system
H
.

Proof 8.1
.

Pick points
z_{i}=(s_{i},t_{i})
in
\mathbb{R}\times[0,1]
so that the map
\ev_{z}
given by evaluation at
z=(z_{0},\ldots,z_{d})
satisfies

\Gamma(u^{*}TX)\supset T_{u}\mathcal{W}_{x_{0},x_{n}}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle D(ev_{z})}}{{\longrightarrow}}\oplus_{i=0}^{d}T_{u(z_{i})}X

is injective. This is an open condition in the
\{z_{i}\}
, so we can perturb and re-index to ensure that the points are also regular and ordered (in particular all the co-ordinates
s_{i}
are distinct). We now pick real codimension one linear subspaces

\tilde{H}_{i}\subset T_{u(z_{i})}X

so that
\oplus_{i}\tilde{H}_{i}\subset\oplus_{i}T_{u(z_{i})}X
is transverse to
\ev_{z}
. We now pick arbitrary open hypersurfaces
H_{i}
with tangent spaces
\tilde{H}_{i}
at
u(z_{i})
. This means that the first condition of Definition
154
holds by construction. Shrinking the
H_{i}
to smaller open subsets if necessary, we can ensure the second condition also holds.

A choice of complete system for
u
gives rise to an open set
u\in V_{\textbf{H}}\subset\mathcal{W}_{x_{0},x_{n}}
with the property that for any
v\in V_{\textbf{H}}
there is a unique point
z_{i}(v)=(s_{i}(v),t_{i})\in\mathbb{R}\times[0,1]
with
v(z_{i}(v))\in H_{i}
, and so that the map

V_{\textbf{H}}\to\mathbb{R}^{d+1},\ v\mapsto\,(s_{0}(v),\ldots,s_{d}(v))

is a diffeomorphism to its image. Taking
V_{\textbf{H}}
to be translation invariant, one gets a a chart
U_{\textbf{H}}
for
\mathcal{W}(x_{0},x_{n})
near
u
with local diffeomorphism

U_{\textbf{H}}\to\mathbb{R}^{d},\ v\mapsto(s_{0}(v)-s_{1}(v),s_{1}(v)-s_{2}(v),\ldots,s_{d-1}(v)-s_{d}(v)).

(16)

The previous lemma implies that such charts cover.

{rmk}

These co-ordinates are similar to the ‘transit time’ co-ordinates used by Barraud and Cornea in
[
BC07
]
, obtained from recording the
s
-co-ordinates of intersections with a collection of level sets of the action functional.

Now let
(u^{1},\ldots,u^{n})\in\mathcal{M}_{x_{0},x_{1}}\times\cdots\times\mathcal{M}_{x_{n-1},x_{n}}
be a broken solution, and for each
1\leq\ell\leq n
fix a complete system
\textbf{H}^{\ell}=\{H^{\ell}_{0},\ldots,H^{\ell}_{\mu(u_{\ell})-1}\}
for
u^{\ell}
. We obtain an open set

U=U_{\textbf{H}^{1}}\times\cdots\times U_{\textbf{H}^{n}}\subset\mathcal{M}_{x_{0},x_{1}}\times\cdots\times\mathcal{M}_{x_{n-1},x_{n}}

A convenient feature of the charts defined by the open sets
U_{\textbf{H}}
associated to complete systems is that they yield ‘associative gluing maps’ in the following sense:

Proposition 156

(
[
Lar
, Corollary 6.8 and Proposition 6.9]
)
.

For sufficiently large
T_{0}\gg 0
there is a gluing map

G:U\times[T_{0},\infty)^{n-1}\to\mathcal{M}_{x_{0},x_{n}}

with the following properties:

1.

it is smooth onto a neighbourhood of the point (at infinity)
(u^{1},\ldots,u^{n})
;

2.

G(u,T)
is the unique trajectory which admits a parametrization with
v(z_{i}^{\ell}(u,T))\in H_{i}^{\ell}
for every
i,\ell
;

3.

gluing is strictly associative in the sense that if we fix
T_{m}\in[T_{0},\infty)
for
m\not\in\{\ell_{1},\ldots,\ell_{r}\}
and further fix

u^{\alpha}=(u^{l_{\alpha-1}+1},\ldots,u^{l_{\alpha}})\in\mathcal{M}_{x_{l_{\alpha-1}},x_{l_{\alpha}}};\quad T^{\alpha}=(T_{l_{\alpha-1}+1},\ldots,T_{l_{\alpha}})

then
G(u,T)=G(G(u^{\alpha},T^{\alpha})_{\alpha=1,\ldots,r},(T_{l_{1}},\ldots,T_{l_{r}}))
.

It is classical that if
w
is obtained from pregluing
(u^{1},\ldots,u^{n})
with gluing lengths
T_{1},\ldots,T_{n-1}
then

\|\overline{\partial}(w)\|_{2,k}\leq C\cdot e^{-\delta\min(\textbf{T})}

for some
C>0
and
\delta>0
. The gluing, obtained from a Newton-Picard iteration, expresses the actual neraby Floer solution as the exponential of a vector field
\xi
along
w
and

\overline{\partial}\exp_{w}(\xi_{\textbf{u,T}})=0,\qquad\|\xi\|_{2,k}\leq C\cdot e^{-\delta\min(\textbf{T})}

(17)

Let
\psi:(0,\varepsilon]\to[T_{0},\infty)
be any diffeomorphism, in this context called a ‘gluing profile’. We apply the map
\psi
co-ordinatewise to vector inputs. The associativity of gluing and usual properties of the gluing map implies that the map

\prod_{\ell}U_{\textbf{H}^{\ell}}\times(0,\varepsilon)^{n-1}\to\mathcal{M}_{x_{0},x_{n}},\qquad(u,\textbf{r})\mapsto G(u,\textbf{T}:=\psi(\textbf{r}))

(18)

extends to a well-defined continuous map

\prod_{\ell}U_{\textbf{H}^{\ell}}\times[0,\varepsilon)^{n-1}\to\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{x_{0},x_{n}}

(19)

Building on exponential decay estimates for Floer solutions,
[
FOOOar
]
proved a smoothness theorem for compactified moduli spaces of holomorphic curves.
In the simple case where the Lagrangians are transverse (so there is no Morse-Bott gluing and there are no weighted Sobolev spaces),
[
Lar
]
gives a simplified proof of the following of their results, by directly analysing the transition functions associated to changing the hypersurfaces in a complete system:

Theorem 157

(Fukaya-Oh-Ohta-Ono
[
FOOOar
]
, Large
[
Lar
, Theorem 6.12]
)
.

If we use the gluing profile
\psi(r)=1/r
then the maps from (
19
) (associated to charts defined by complete systems of hypersurfaces) give
\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{x_{0},x_{n}}
the structure of a smooth manifold with corners.

Proof 8.2

(Sketch)
.

The crucial decay estimate refines (
17
) by showing that

\|(\nabla_{\textbf{u}})^{p}\frac{\partial^{m}}{\partial\textbf{T}^{m}}(\xi_{\textbf{u,T}})|_{[-S,S]\times[0,1]}\|_{2,k-p-|m|}\leq C\cdot e^{-\delta\min(\textbf{T})}\leq Ce^{-\delta^{\prime}/\max(\textbf{r})},\qquad p+|m|\leq k-1

(20)

where the last inequality uses the choice of gluing profile and
\delta^{\prime}<\delta
is chosen appropriately.

Given two complete systems of hypersurfaces
H
and
H’
, the transition map between associated charts has the shape, cf. (
16
)

(u,\textbf{r})\mapsto((\tilde{s}_{i}(u,\textbf{r})-\tilde{s}_{i-1}(u,\textbf{r})),\tilde{\textbf{r}})

for the ‘new’ transit lengths
\tilde{s}_{j}
and functions schematically of the form
\tilde{r}_{j}=r_{j}/(1+\phi(r,\tilde{s}_{i}-s_{i})
. Smoothness of gluing amounts to the fact that the functions
\tilde{s}_{i}
extend smoothly over
\textbf{r}=(0,\ldots,0)
, which is inferred from (
20
).

{rmk}

The map
\psi(r)=-\ln(r)
does not yield a smooth manifold-with-corners structure, even though this is a ‘natural’ profile from the viewpoint of algebraic geometry (essentially because the modulus of the annulus
\{1<|z|<r\}
is
-\ln(r)/2\pi
and that modulus is closely related to the smoothing parameter
\{xy=\varepsilon\}
for a node).

The discussion up to this point adapts directly to prove that compactified regular moduli spaces of Floer cylinders are smooth manifolds with corners, using local charts which again record differences of the
s
-co-ordinates of intersections with real hypersurfaces
H_{i}
where the co-ordinates lie in the cylinder rather than the strip, in direct analogy with (
16
).

8.2
Smooth structures on other moduli spaces

For moduli spaces of holomorphic triangles, where the domain
\Sigma
is rigid and all breaking is unstable, the previous analysis applies but one must first choose a parametrisation
\mathbb{R}\times(a,b)\to\Sigma
of an open subset of the domain and work with the corresponding local co-ordinates, which allow one to define a regular point as in (
15
).

The remaining step is to deal with moduli spaces where the underlying domain moves in a non-trivial moduli space
\mathcal{R}
of holomorphic curves. The only cases relevant to this paper are where
\mathcal{R}=[0,1]
, namely a moduli space of four-marked discs, used in constructing the ‘associator’ which proves associativity of the composition operation on bilinear flow morphisms (i.e. of the Floer product), and a moduli space of discs with two boundary marked points and an interior marked point, arising in the discussion of open-closed maps.

Let
\mathcal{F}(x_{\alpha})
denote a compactified moduli space of solutions
(p,u)
where
u:C_{p}\to X
is defined on a Riemann surface
C_{p}
depending on a point
p\in\mathcal{R}\simeq[0,1]
(and the
x_{\alpha}
denote the fixed asymptotics, i.e. corners of holomorphic polygons and/or an interior Hamiltonian orbit). Recall that regularity of the moduli space (with varying
p
) then concerns surjectivity of an extended linearised operator

D_{C_{p},u}^{\ext}:\,T_{p}\mathcal{R}\oplus W^{1,2}(C_{p},u^{*}TX)\to L^{2}(\Omega^{0,1}\otimes_{J}u^{*}TX)

which takes account of variation of the domain parameter, and there is a natural map

\ker(D_{C_{p},u}^{\ext})=T_{C_{p},u}\mathcal{F}(x_{\alpha})\to T_{p}\mathcal{R}

(21)

coming from the forgetful map
\mathrm{forget}:\mathcal{F}(x_{\alpha})\to\mathcal{R}
. There are now two different possibilities: either (
21
) is trivial, or it is surjective. Accordingly, we introduce two kinds of charts on the moduli space. Suppose
\dim\mathcal{F}(x_{\alpha})=d
:

1.

Suppose (
21
) vanishes. This means we are in a situation much as before, since the tangent space to
\mathcal{F}(x_{\alpha})
at
(p,u)
is spanned by variations with fixed domain. Choose
d
points
z_{1},\ldots,z_{d}\subset C_{p}
lying in a chart
\mathbb{R}\times(a,b)\subset C_{p}
(for instance in a the strip-like ends), regular in the sense of (
15
) for the given co-ordinates, and open hypersurfaces
H_{1},\ldots,H_{d}\subset X
with
u(z_{i})\in H_{i}
so that

\partial_{s}u|_{z_{i}}\neq 0,\ u^{-1}(H_{i})\pitchfork\partial_{s}\ \textrm{at}\ z_{i}.

and

\ev_{\textbf{z}}:\mathcal{F}(x_{\alpha})\to X^{d}

is transverse to
H_{1}\times\cdots\times H_{d}
at
(p,u)
. We obtain an open set
U_{\textbf{H}}\subset\mathcal{F}(x_{\alpha})
with co-ordinates so that for
v\in U_{\textbf{H}}
there are points
z_{i}(v)=(s_{i}(v),t_{i})
in the strip-like-end co-ordinates with
v(z_{i}(v))\in H_{i}
and
v\mapsto(s_{1}(v),\ldots,s_{d}(v))\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
defines a local chart.

2.

Suppose instead that (
21
) is onto at
(p,u)
. Again fix a chart
\mathbb{R}\times(a,b)\subset C_{p}
. We now pick points
z_{1},\ldots,z_{d-1}\subset C_{p}
in the chart, pick codimension one open hypersurfaces
H_{1},\ldots,H_{d-1}
of
X
containing the
u(z_{i})
, regular in the previous sense, and finally pick a codimension one hypersurface
X\times\{p\}=H_{\mathcal{R}}\subset X\times\mathcal{R}
, and ask that

\ev_{\textbf{z}}\times(\mathrm{forget}):\mathcal{F}(x_{\alpha})\to X^{d-1}\times\mathcal{R}

is transverse to
H_{1}\times\cdots\times H_{d-1}\times H_{\mathcal{R}}
at
(p,u)
. This yields an open set
U_{(\beta,\textbf{H})}\subset\mathcal{F}(x_{\alpha})
where nearby solutions
v
are uniquely determined by the
s
-co-ordinates
s_{i}(v)
of the local intersections with the
H_{i}
together with their intersection with
H_{\mathcal{R}}
, which in particular records the conformal parameter of the domain at
v
.

{rmk}

Both cases involve picking
(d-1)
hypersurfaces in
X
and one in
X\times\mathcal{R}
and asking for transversality of an evaluation map to
X^{d}\times\mathcal{R}
; the final hypersurface is
H_{d}\times\mathcal{R}
in the first case and
X\times\{p\}
in the second.
For spaces
\mathcal{R}
of domains which are higher-dimensional manifolds with corners, the crucial information is the rank
r
of (
21
), and one should construct charts which involve picking
r
codimension one hypersurfaces in the product
X\times\mathcal{R}
, but we will stick to the simpler case which suffices for our purposes.

Lemma 158
.

Charts of the previous two kinds cover the moduli space
\mathcal{F}(x_{\alpha})
.

Proof 8.3
.

We have
T_{(p,u)}\mathcal{F}(x_{\alpha})\subset\Gamma(u^{*}TX)\oplus T_{p}\mathcal{R}
. For linear hyperplanes
\tilde{H}_{i}\subset T_{u(z_{i})}(X)
, let
W^{2}(\underline{\tilde{H}})
denote those vector fields
\xi\in u^{*}TX
with
\xi(z_{i})\in\tilde{H}_{i}
, where
1\leq i\leq\{d-1,d\}
so this has codimension
d
respectively
d-1
depending on whether the chart has the first or second form. For suitably chosen hyperplanes, as in Lemma
155
, a complement to the kernel
\ker(D^{ext}
is given by
T_{p}\mathcal{R}\oplus W^{2}(\underline{\tilde{H}})
in the first case and
W^{2}(\underline{\tilde{H}})
in the second. The proof then follows as before, by taking generic hyperplanes
H_{i}
in
X
tangent to the
\tilde{H}_{i}
.

{ex}

Suppose
p
lies in a collar neighbourhood of the boundary of
\mathcal{R}
covered by the image of a gluing map
\beta
. The proof of linear gluing shows that (
21
) is onto, so a neighbourhood of the Gromov-Floer boundary is covered by charts of the second kind. The domain
C_{p}
contains a distinguished ‘neck’, coming from the gluing, and it is natural to take the
z_{i}
in that neck. Nearby solutions
v
are uniquely determined by the
s
-co-ordinates
s_{i}(v)
together with the unique value
\beta(v)
of the gluing parameter (i.e. collar co-ordinate) at
v
.

We use the same gluing profile
\psi(r)=1/r
to map the parameter space
[L_{0},\infty)
for neck length (equivalently conformal parameter near the boundary in
\mathcal{R}\simeq[0,1]
) to an interval
(0,\varepsilon]
. With this choice:

Proposition 159
.

The previous charts endow the compactified moduli space
\mathcal{F}(x_{\alpha})
with the structure of a smooth manifold with corners.

Proof 8.4

(Sketch)
.

The proof follows the lines of Theorem
157
. The interior of the moduli space is canonically smooth, so we need only consider smoothness near the boundary. For unstable breaking the situation is exactly as in
op. cit
. Also for stable breaking, the transition functions associated to nested strata in the boundary, with charts defined by a fixed choice of hyperplanes (and perhaps a neck length), are smooth because of the strict associativity of gluing, which follows from the geometric characterisation of the co-ordinates in the two kinds of chart. The new phenomenon arises when considering charts near a boundary stratum corresponding to stable breaking, when one either compares a chart involving only hypersurfaces
\{H_{i}\}
and one with a neck length
\{H_{i}^{\prime},\beta\}
, or compares two charts involving neck lengths
\beta,\beta^{\prime}
. In the first case, the estimate (
20
) again shows that the length
\beta
depends smoothly on the co-ordinates associated to the
\{H_{i}\}
. In the second case, the transition function is smooth in the neck length parameter since that is
intrinsic
to
\mathcal{F}(x_{\alpha})
, being determined by the conformal structure of the domain.

8.3
Index bundles

At a regular Floer solution
u:\mathbb{R}\times[0,1]\to X
with transverse Lagrangian boundary conditions
L,K
there is a linearised operator
D_{u}=D(\overline{\partial}_{J}|_{u})
, whose kernel
\ker(D_{u})
is the fibre of the tangent bundle
T\mathcal{W}_{xy}\to\mathcal{W}_{xy}
to the space of Floer solutions (where we have not quotiented by the re-parametrisation action of
\mathbb{R}
). Over the open locus of unbroken solutions, the map
\mathcal{W}_{xy}\to\mathcal{M}_{xy}=\mathcal{W}_{xy}/\mathbb{R}
admits a unique section up to contractible choice, which induces a bundle
\mathrm{Ind}(x,y)\to\mathcal{M}_{xy}
called the
index bundle
.

Lemma 160
.

The index bundle
\mathrm{Ind}(x,y)\to\mathcal{M}_{xy}
extends as a topological vector bundle to the compactification
\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{xy}
. Moreover, one can construct this extension in such a way that there are gluing isomorphisms
\psi=\psi_{xyz}:\mathrm{Ind}(x,y)\oplus\mathrm{Ind}(y,z)\to\mathrm{Ind}(x,z)
over
\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{xyz}
which are associative, meaning the following diagram commutes over
\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{xyzw}
.

\textstyle{\mathrm{Ind}(x,y)\oplus\mathrm{Ind}(y,z)\oplus\mathrm{Ind}(z,w)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathrm{Ind}(x,y)\oplus\mathrm{Ind}(y,w)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathrm{Ind}(x,z)\oplus\mathrm{Ind}(z,w)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathrm{Ind}(x,w)}

Proof 8.5
.

See
[
Lar
, Proposition 7.3]
.

Suppose we now consider a space of holomorphic curves which are maps from a family
\mathcal{R}
of domains; then the tangent bundle to the moduli space at
(C_{p},u)
with
p\in\mathcal{R}
and
u:C_{p}\to X
is given by the kernel of the extended operator
\ker(D_{(C_{p},u)}^{\mathrm{ext}})
introduced previously.

8.4
Index bundles and abstract index bundles

The index bundles
\mathrm{Ind}(x,y)
do not canonically contain
T\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{xy}
. What we instead have are short exact sequences

0\rightarrow\mathbb{R}\tau_{xy}\xrightarrow{\sigma_{xy}}\mathrm{Ind}(x,y)\rightarrow T\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{xy}\to 0

where the first arrow comes from the
\mathbb{R}
-symmetry on the set of Cauchy-Riemann sections.

At a regular point
u
of a moduli space of Floer solutions with asymptotics
x,y
, the index bundle
\mathrm{Ind}(x,y)
is the tangent space to the space of Floer solutions,
\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{xy}
is the compactification of the space of solutions modulo reparametrization, and the translation vector field
\partial/\partial s
generates the
\mathbb{R}
-factor of
\mathrm{Ind}(x,y)
. If
u_{1}\in\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{xy}
and
u_{2}\in\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{yz}
are Floer solutions, then (after suitable translation and cutting-off) there is a parameter-dependent gluing
u_{R}=u_{1}\#_{R}u_{2}
(
R
being the gluing parameter). Linear gluing (cf.
[
Sei08
, Chapter II.12.f]
) shows that under the gluing map,

((\partial/\partial s)_{u_{1}},(\partial/\partial s)_{u_{2}})\mapsto(\partial/\partial s)_{u_{R}},\quad(-(\partial/\partial s)_{u_{1}},(\partial/\partial s)_{u_{2}})\mapsto v

where
v\in\mathrm{Ind}(x,z)/\mathbb{R}
has non-trivial inward-pointing component to the boundary face (i.e. in the collar direction of the embedding
\mathcal{C}:\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{xy}\times\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{yz}\times[0,\varepsilon)\to\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{xz}
).
It follows that the index bundles further satisfy that the following diagram (cf.
[
Lar
, (7.17)]
) commutes

\textstyle{0\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathbb{R}\tau_{xz}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\sigma_{xz}}
\scriptstyle{\Delta}
\textstyle{\mathrm{Ind}(x,z)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\psi}
\textstyle{T\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{xz}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{0}
\textstyle{0\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathbb{R}\tau_{xy}\oplus\mathbb{R}\tau_{yz}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{(\sigma_{xy},\sigma_{yz})}
\textstyle{\mathrm{Ind}(x,y)\oplus\mathrm{Ind}(y,z)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{T\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{xy}\oplus T\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{yz}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{0}

where
\Delta
is the diagonal map sending
\tau_{xz}
to
\tau_{xy}+\tau_{yz}
,
\psi
is the (analytically defined) linear gluing map and the rightmost vertical map is a choice of splitting for the natural map

dc:T\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{xy}\oplus T\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{yz}\rightarrow T\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{xz}

The composition
\mathbb{R}\tau_{xy}\oplus\mathbb{R}\tau_{yz}\to T\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{xz}
also sends
\tau_{xyz}
to an inwards-pointing normal vector in
T\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{xz}
for the boundary component
\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{xy}\times\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{yz}
.

By splitting the short exact sequence above, there are isomorphisms
\mathrm{Ind}(x,y)\simeq T\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{xy}\oplus\mathbb{R}\tau_{xy}
. We would like to choose such isomorphisms which are compatible with the abstract gluing isomorphisms for the right hand side.

Proposition 161
.

Let
\overline{\mathcal{M}}=\overline{\mathcal{M}}^{LK}
be the flow category associated to a pair of spectral Lagrangian branes. Then there are isomorphisms of vector bundles

\phi=\phi_{xy}:\mathrm{Ind}(x,y)\to I_{xy}

where
I_{xy}=I_{xy}^{\mathcal{M}}
is the abstract index bundle of
\overline{\mathcal{M}}
, and the
\phi_{xy}
respect the abstract and analytic gluing isomorphisms, in the sense that the following diagram commutes:

\textstyle{\mathrm{Ind}(x,y)\oplus\mathrm{Ind}(y,z)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\psi}
\scriptstyle{\phi}
\textstyle{\mathrm{Ind}(x,z)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\phi}
\textstyle{I_{xy}\oplus I_{yz}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\psi}
\textstyle{I_{xz}}

and are compatible with projection to tangent spaces, in the sense that the following diagram commutes

\textstyle{\mathrm{Ind}(x,y)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\phi}
\textstyle{I_{xy}=T\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{xy}\oplus\mathbb{R}\tau_{xy}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{T\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{xy}}

where the horizontal arrow is projection onto the first factor.

Furthermore, this
\phi
is canonical up to contractible choice.

Proof 8.6
.

We construct the
\phi_{xy}
inductively in the dimension of the
\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{xy}
. Assume we have constructed suitably associative
\phi_{xy}
when
|x|-|y|-1<k
and pick
x,z
such that
|x|-|z|-1=k
. Then we want to choose
\phi_{z}
such that the following diagram commutes over
\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{xyz}
for all
y
.

\textstyle{\mathrm{Ind}(x,y)\oplus\mathrm{Ind}(y,z)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\psi}
\textstyle{\mathrm{Ind}(x,z)}
\textstyle{I_{xy}\oplus I_{yz}=\mathbb{R}\tau_{xy}\oplus T\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{xy}\oplus\mathbb{R}\tau_{yz}\oplus T\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{yz}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
