\scriptstyle{\psi}
\textstyle{I_{xz}=\mathbb{R}\tau_{xz}\oplus T\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{xz}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\phi^{-1}_{xz}}

This determines
\phi_{xz}
over all
\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{xyz}
(and these are compatible over all overlaps
\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{xyy^{\prime}z}
). Note that this diagram is compatible with the projection maps to
T\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{xy}\oplus T\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{yz}
and
T\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{xz}
, by
[
Lar
, (7.17)]
. We then choose a splitting
T\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{xz}\to\mathrm{Ind}_{xz}
extending the one on the boundary to define
\phi_{xz}^{-1}
over
T\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{xz}\subseteq I_{xz}
(which we can do since the space of such splittings is convex), and choose
\phi_{xz}^{-1}(\tau_{xz})
to be transverse to the subspace
\phi_{xz}^{-1}T\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{xz}
and in the same component of such vectors as the image of the analytically-defined map
\mathbb{R}\to\mathrm{Ind}_{xz}
(again, we can do this since the space of such transverse vectors is convex; note the reason we don’t take
\phi^{-1}(\tau_{xz})
to be equal to the analytically-defined map
\mathbb{R}\to\mathrm{Ind}_{xz}
is because this would be incompatible with commutativity of the required diagrams).

The choices we made above are canonical up to contractible choice so it follows that
\phi
is too.

{rmk}

Proposition
161
would be false if we further required that the following diagram commutes:

\textstyle{\mathbb{R}\tau_{xy}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\sigma_{xy}}
\textstyle{\mathrm{Ind}(x,y)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\phi}
\textstyle{I_{xy}}

where the map
\mathbb{R}\tau_{xy}\to I_{xy}=\mathbb{R}\tau_{xy}\oplus T\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{xy}
is the inclusion into the first factor.

We have an analogue of the previous result for continuation maps rather than morphisms. Let
\mathcal{F}
,
\mathcal{G}
be two flow categories from Floer theory. We already have isomorphisms
\phi:\mathrm{Ind}^{\mathcal{F}}_{xx^{\prime}}\simeq I^{\mathcal{F}}_{xx^{\prime}}
compatible with gluing on both sides; there is an analogous version for morphisms of flow categories from Floer theory:

Lemma 162
.

Let
\mathcal{W}:\mathcal{F}\to\mathcal{G}
be a morphism of Floer flow categories
\mathcal{F}=\overline{\mathcal{M}}^{LK}
,
\mathcal{G}=\overline{\mathcal{M}}^{LK^{\prime}}
associated to a continuation map of spectral Lagrangian branes from
K
to
K^{\prime}
. Then there are isomorphisms
\phi:I^{\mathcal{W}}_{xy}\to\mathrm{Ind}(x,y)\oplus\mathbb{R}
such that the following diagrams commute:

\textstyle{I^{\mathcal{W}}_{xy}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\phi}
\textstyle{\mathrm{Ind}^{\mathcal{W}}(x,y)\oplus\mathbb{R}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{T\mathcal{W}_{xy}}

\textstyle{I_{xx^{\prime}}^{\mathcal{F}}\oplus I^{\mathcal{W}}_{x^{\prime}y}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\psi}
\scriptstyle{\phi\oplus\phi}
\textstyle{I^{\mathcal{W}}_{xy}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\phi}
\textstyle{\mathrm{Ind}^{\mathcal{F}}(x,x^{\prime})\oplus\mathrm{Ind}(x^{\prime},y)\oplus\mathbb{R}\sigma_{x^{\prime}y}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\psi}
\textstyle{\mathrm{Ind}(x,y)\oplus\mathbb{R}\sigma_{xy}}

and
similar for breaking on the right, where the
\sigma
s are generators for the extra copies of
\mathbb{R}
and
\psi
acts as the identity on them.

Proof 8.7
.

Assume we’ve chosen
\phi
whenever
|x|-|y|<k
. Pick
x,y
such that
|x|-|y|=k
. We want to choose
\phi_{xy}
such that the diagrams commute on appropriate boundary faces. (Note that by associativity, where this compatibility is enforced twice (i.e. over the codimension 2 boundary faces), these are compatible with each other). Therefore
\phi_{xy}
is already specified over the boundary; these come from some splittings
\bar{\phi}:T\mathcal{W}_{xy}\to\mathbb{R}\sigma_{xy}
. Choose
\bar{\phi}
extending this over the boundary; choose some map
\mathbb{R}\tau_{xy}\to\mathrm{Ind}^{\mathcal{W}}(x,y)\oplus\mathbb{R}
which is transverse to
\phi_{xy}(T\mathcal{W}_{xy})
and in the same component of such things as the identity
\mathbb{R}\to\mathbb{R}\sigma_{xy}
extending that on the boundary, and take
\phi_{xy}
to be given by
\bar{\phi}
on the subspace
\mathbb{R}\tau_{xy}
.

Let
\mathcal{Z}=\mathcal{M}^{LKMN}
be an associator from Section
7.4
. The moduli space of domains is
\mathcal{R}\simeq[0,1]
, and recall there is an analytic index bundle

\mathrm{Ind}^{\mathcal{Z}}(x,y,z;w)

(22)

over each
\mathcal{Z}_{xyz;w}
, whose fibre at a map
u:C_{p}\to X
is given by the kernel of (a virtual perturbation of) the (pointwise) Cauchy-Riemann operator over
C_{p}
, as in
[
Lar
, Section 7.3]
.

In this case, there is also an extended Cauchy-Riemann operator
D^{\mathrm{Ext}}
(see Section
8.2
and
[
Lar
, (5.3)]
); which is surjective (by the assumption that the moduli spaces are transversely cut out) with kernel
T\mathcal{Z}_{xyz;w}
.

The same proof as in the cases of Floer flow categories and continuation maps shows that there are isomorphisms of vector bundles:

\phi:I^{\mathcal{Z}}_{xyz;w}\to\ker(D^{\mathrm{Ext}})\oplus\mathbb{R}

which are compatible with the abstract and analytic gluing isomorphisms on each side over each boundary face, such that the following diagram commutes:

\textstyle{I^{\mathcal{Z}}_{xyz;w}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\phi}
\textstyle{\ker(D^{\mathrm{Ext}})\oplus\mathbb{R}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{T\mathcal{Z}_{xyz;w}}

where both downwards arrows are projection maps.

Using consistent surjective perturbations
D_{u}+f
of the pointwise operators (adding finite-dimensional spaces to the domains of the operators), Large
[
Lar
, Section 8.5]
constructs stable isomorphisms

\ker(D_{(C_{p},u)}^{\mathrm{Ext}})\simeq\ker(D_{u}+f)\oplus\mathbb{R}

which are compatible with breaking, and where the
\mathbb{R}
comes from a choice of trivialisation of
T\mathcal{R}
.

8.5
Stable framings from spaces of caps

We outline Large’s approach to constructing the data used to frame a Floer flow category.
Let
X
be an exact symplectic manifold with a stable trivialisation

\Psi:TX\oplus\mathbb{C}^{N-n}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\longrightarrow}}\mathbb{C}^{N}

and let
L
and
K
be transverse graded Lagrangian branes which are more explicitly equipped with Lagrangian subbundles

\Gamma_{L}\subset L\times[0,1]\times\mathbb{C}^{N}

with
(\Gamma_{L})|_{L\times\{0\}}=\Psi(TL\oplus\mathbb{R}^{N-n})
and
(\Gamma_{L})|_{L\times\{1\}}=\mathbb{R}^{N}
, and similarly for
K
. Let
x\in\mathcal{M}^{LK}
be an intersection point of
L
and
K
. We will associate a stable vector space
V(x)
to
x
as follows. In the case where
L,K
are not transverse, one can incorporate a Hamiltonian
H_{t}
into the Floer data so that
L\pitchfork\phi_{H_{t}}^{1}(K)
and take
x(t)
a Hamiltonian chord; since the analogous case of Hamiltonian orbits involves this
t
-dependence we will incorporate it into the notation.

Let
Z=\mathbb{R}\times[0,1]
with co-ordinates
(s,t)
.
The linearisation of the
\overline{\partial}
-operator at the constant Floer solution
u(s,t)=x=x(t)
has the form

\xi\mapsto\overline{\partial}_{J_{t}}(\xi)+Y_{t}(\xi)

(23)

for a translation-invariant one-form
Y_{t}\in\Omega^{0,1}(Z)\otimes_{\mathbb{C}}x^{*}TX
(this is usually written explicitly with respect to a choice of symplectic connection on
TX
). By taking the direct sum with a trivial
\overline{\partial}
-operator on
\mathbb{C}^{N-n}
(not changing the kernel or cokernel of the linearisation), we get an operator

D_{x}:W^{1,2}(Z,\mathbb{C}^{N},T_{x}L\oplus\mathbb{R}^{N-n},T_{x}K\oplus\mathbb{R}^{N-n})\longrightarrow L^{2}(Z,\Omega^{0,1}\otimes_{J}\mathbb{C}^{N}).

(24)

Let
D_{+}=D^{2}\backslash\{-1\}
. We now fix the following data:

1.

a strip-like end
\varepsilon:(-\infty,0]\times[0,1]\hookrightarrow D_{+}
at the puncture;

2.

a real
L>0
and an almost complex structure
J
on
\mathbb{C}^{N}\times D_{+}
(taming the standard symplectic structure) and one-form
Y\in\Omega^{0,1}(D_{+})\otimes_{J}\mathrm{End}(\mathbb{C}^{N})
which agree with
J_{t},Y_{t}
from (
23
) on the image of
\varepsilon|_{(-\infty,-L)\times[0,1])}
;

3.

a Lagrangian subbundle
\Gamma\subset\partial D_{+}\times[0,1]\times\mathbb{C}^{N}
which interpolates between the pairs
(T_{x}L\oplus\mathbb{R}^{N-n},T_{x}K\oplus\mathbb{R}^{N-n})
on
\varepsilon(-\infty,-L)\times\{0,1\})
at
\{0\}
and the constant
\mathbb{R}^{N}
at
\{1\}
, and which agrees with
\Gamma_{L}
respectively
\Gamma_{K}
on
\varepsilon((-\infty,-L)\times\{0\})\times[0,1]
respectively
\varepsilon((-\infty,-L)\times\{1\})\times[0,1]
.

The data specifies an abstract real Cauchy-Riemann operator

D:W^{1,2}(D_{+},\mathbb{C}^{N},\Lambda|_{D_{+}\times\{0\}})\to L^{2}(D_{+},\Omega^{0,1}(D_{+})\otimes_{J}\mathbb{C}^{N})

(25)

We can fix a
q\geq 0
and a linear map
f:\mathbb{R}^{q}\to C^{\infty}(Z,\Omega^{0,1}(D_{+})\otimes_{J}\mathbb{C}^{N})
(with image in the space of sections vanishing over the image of
\varepsilon
) so that
D+f
is surjective, and then define

V_{q}(x):=\ker(D+f)-\mathbb{R}^{q}.

Clearly stabilising by increasing
q
and replacing
f
by
\mathbb{R}^{q+1}\to\mathbb{R}^{q}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle f}}{{\longrightarrow}}C^{\infty}(Z,\Omega^{0,1}(D_{+})\otimes_{J}\mathbb{C}^{N})
yields an isomorphic stable vector space.

Since the flow category
\mathcal{M}^{LK}
is finite, we can pick a sufficiently large
q
to make the operator
D=D_{x}
surjective for all
x\in L\pitchfork\phi_{H_{t}}^{1}(K)
simultaneously.

Proposition 163

(
[
Lar
, Section 8.4]
)
.

The association
x\mapsto V_{q}(x)
has the following features:

1.

Up to homotopy, the space of choices made in constructing
V_{q}(x)
is given by the data of a sufficiently large
q
;

2.

for
q\gg 0
there are stable isomorphisms
V_{q}(x)\oplus\mathrm{Ind}(x,y)\to V_{q}(y)
which are compatible with gluing, in the sense that the following diagram of vector bundles over
\mathcal{M}_{xy}\times\mathcal{M}_{yz}
commutes:

\textstyle{V_{q}(x)\oplus\mathrm{Ind}(x,y)\oplus\mathrm{Ind}(y,z)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{V_{q}(y)\oplus\mathrm{Ind}(y,z)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{V_{q}(x)\oplus\mathrm{Ind}(x,z)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{V_{q}(z)}

Assuming we have taken a sufficiently large such
q
, we will suppress the
q
and just write
V_{x}
for the stable framing space associated to
x
.

{rmk}

Since in the proof of the theorem we only need to consider moduli spaces of holomorphic curves of dimension
\leq n+1
, for our purposes it would suffice to choose a sufficiently large
q
such that all the spaces of choices involved are sufficiently highly connected, but not necessarily contractible.

The choices enumerated previously yield a contractible space
\mathcal{U}(x)
of ‘abstract half-planes’ carrying a (trivial) stable bundle given by the index of (
25
). The proof of the Proposition relies on a gluing operation
\mathcal{U}(x)\times\mathcal{M}^{LK}_{xy}\to\mathcal{U}(y)
(strictly defined on a relatively compact open subset of the domain determined by the collar structure) which is associative up to coherent homotopy.

If
\mathcal{W}:\mathcal{F}\to\mathcal{G}
is a continuation morphism of Floer flow categories associated to a Hamiltonian isotopy, there is a parallel story, except the gluing theory that underlies the isomorphism
\mathrm{Ind}(x,y)\oplus\mathcal{V}(y)\simeq\mathcal{U}(x)
with
\mathrm{Ind}(x,y)=T\mathcal{W}(x,y)
relates spaces of caps
\mathcal{U}(x)
and
\mathcal{V}(y)
for
\mathcal{F}
respectively
\mathcal{G}
with a space
\mathcal{W}(x,y)
of Floer continuation solutions.

We briefly comment on the analogous results for other moduli spaces:

1.

For a framed bilinear map
\mathcal{R}:\mathcal{F}\times\mathcal{G}\to\mathcal{H}
there is a (suitably associative) gluing map

\mathcal{U}(x)\times\mathcal{U}(y)\times\mathcal{R}_{xy;z}\longrightarrow\mathcal{U}(z)

which covers an isomorphism of index bundles

V_{x}\oplus V_{y}\oplus I^{\mathcal{R}}_{xy;z}\longrightarrow V_{z}\oplus\mathbb{R};

2.

for a space of discs
\mathcal{M}_{x}
with one incoming puncture (as used in constructing units), there is an inclusion
\mathcal{M}_{x}\hookrightarrow\mathcal{U}(x)
which identifies the index bundle
I_{x}
over
\mathcal{M}_{x}
with the restriction of
V_{x}
;

3.

for a space of discs
\mathcal{M}_{\bar{x}}
with a unique
outgoing
puncture (as arise in constructing co-units), there is a gluing map
\mathcal{U}(x)\times\mathcal{M}_{\bar{x}}\to\mathcal{D}
where
\mathcal{D}
denotes a space of perturbed holomorphic discs on
L
with no boundary punctures. By turning off all Hamiltonian perturbation data, and using exactness, the inclusion of constant discs
L\to\mathcal{D}
is a deformation retraction. The stable framing on
L
then gives a stable equivalence between
I_{\bar{x}}
and
-V_{x}
.

4.

For an associator
\mathcal{Z}
defined by a space
\scrR\simeq[0,1]
of holomorphic discs with four boundary punctures, there are gluing maps

\mathcal{U}(x)\times\mathcal{U}(y)\times\mathcal{U}(z)\times\mathcal{Z}_{xyz;w}\longrightarrow\mathcal{U}(w).

Then the stable isomorphisms from Proposition
163
, combined with the comparisons between the analytic and abstract index bundles from Section
8.4
, provide the data required for the framings in Section
7
.

{rmk}

In Proposition
163
, the stable isomorphisms exist with trivial stabilising bundle, coming from the choice of a sufficiently large
q
when constructing the stable vector space
V(x)=\ker(D+f)-\mathbb{R}^{q}
. That is, once all operators have been stabilised to be surjective, the gluing maps
\mathcal{U}(x)\times\mathcal{M}_{xy}\to\mathcal{U}(y)
are covered by isomorphisms of actual vector bundles which on fibres are given by
L^{2}
-orthogonal projections on pregluings, schematically
\ker(D_{u}+f)+\ker(D_{v})\to\ker(D_{u\#v}+g)
for appropriate stabilisations
f,g
. In general, in all the situations we encounter, by making the original stabilisations sufficiently large (for all
x
and over all relatively compact subsets of spaces of operators
\mathcal{U}(x)
and
\mathcal{D}
encountered in homotopies from gluing, or between compositions of continuation maps and the identity, etc), we can ensure that no stabilising bundles appear in the analytic construction. However, the general abstract framework we developed in Section
4.3
does require the flexibility of allowing non-trivial stabilising bundles, for instance in constructing inverses in
\Flow
.
